## 2026-10-16 · plato-sand-dam

# Plato · 16 Oct 2026 · daily Reel package
Hidden Stories of Ashtanga (no Ep. number until filmed; last posted Ep. 4)
"The Old Man Who Threw Sand in the River"

Route: Lane A (Hidden Stories). Last 3 pieces: tortoise script 15 Oct (Lane A, not filmed), Bhagiratha script 14 Oct (Lane A, not filmed), Mysore Reel moved to Mon 19 Oct (Lane B). Next real post is Lane B, so this one is on route.
Habit: #4 Sleep (careful words, no promise) + the core habit (same practice, rest on the rest day).
Direction check: this is the "no equipment race" point (cold plunges) told kindly. We never attack the ice bath. We show the gap.

Trigger note: the only unused life note (13 Oct, the hypermobility jab) still needs his own words, so not used. No @Plato request in the team chat.
News picked: ScienceDaily, 14 Oct 2026, "Scientists reveal why some workout recovery tricks can backfire" (from The Conversation, by Max Davis, University of Bath). It is an expert summary of past studies, not a new trial. What it says:
- Ice baths can ease soreness. But the swelling after training also helps build muscle. Ice baths used a lot "may limit some of the long-term benefits of training". Best only when you must recover fast.
- Massage: people feel less sore. Real performance gains: small and not steady.
- Supplements: some small help, lots of hype. Food is better.
- What works best: sleep 7 to 9 hours, enough protein and food, a sensible plan with rest days.
- Last line: "recovery cannot be bottled and sold."
Never say "ice baths make you weak". Say "may take back some of what you trained for".

## The link (4 lines, told to a 10-year-old)
- Trigger: scientists looked at ice baths, massage and recovery drinks. The best recovery was the simple stuff: sleep, food, rest days.
- Story: a young man wants to know all the holy books, fast, with no teacher. A very old man comes to the river and throws in sand, handful by handful, to build a dam. "You can't stop a river with sand!" "Yes. Same as you. Go learn from a teacher." (Mahabharata, Vana Parva 135)
- Behavior: we buy the fast way. The slow, simple way is the one that works.
- Asana: Savasana, "take rest", at the end of every class. And Saturday, the rest day. Free. Nobody can sell it to you.

Point in one line a child can repeat: "You can't buy rest. You have to take it."

Toolkit pick: Boonchu's "Wrong Fix" skeleton (if you train hard and want to recover, more ice is not the fix, sleep and the rest day are) + /story 4 beats. Hook lever: Contrarian. Why: the last two days were Question-First and Curiosity Amplifiers, so this rotates. The wrong fix is something the viewer really does (ice, massage gun, recovery drink), so the mirror moment comes in second 2. The answer (sleep, rest) waits to the end.
Other options: (1) Pattern interrupt: Boonchu lies flat in Savasana, eyes closed, and says "This is my ice bath." Then the story. (2) Two-Voice Deadpan: buzzword voice "Cold plunge. Recovery drink. Massage gun." pause, flat: "Or... sleep."

## Hooks (/hook, answered myself)
Topic: the best recovery is simple and free; shortcuts can cost you.
Consensus: "Serious people do ice baths, massage guns and recovery drinks. More recovery tools = faster progress."
Where Boonchu sharpens it: 20 years of Ashtanga, six days a week since 2008. The method has had a rest day (Saturday) and moon days off, and every practice ends lying down. He has not needed a cold plunge to come back every morning.
1. Authority: "20 years, six days a week. My recovery tool cost nothing."
2. Contrarian: "Ice bath after every workout? It may take back what you trained."
3. Stakes: "Every day ice bath... maybe you lose some of the strong you built."
4. Question: "Ice bath, massage, recovery drink. Which one really works?"
5. Pattern interrupt: (lying in Savasana, eyes closed) "This is my ice bath."
Pick: 2 at 0-3 s (on screen: "Ice bath every day? 🧊 Careful."), then 4 as the second hook at 3-6 s ("So what really works? Scientists check."). Why: it hits something the viewer did this week, it is a little scary, and the answer is held to the end (REVEAL AT THE END). 10-year test: a child understands "ice may take back your muscle".
Cover: "Ice bath? Careful." with "Careful" in gold. 3 words.

## Script (/story, 60 s, spoken to camera, his spoken English)
HOOK (0-6 s)
"You do ice bath after every training?
Careful. It maybe take back some of what you train for."
(pause) "So what really work? Scientists check. Wait. First, a story."
On screen: 🧊 every day? · ⚠️

CONTEXT (6-14 s)
"Bedtime story. Long time ago, a young man want to know all the holy books.
But learning from a teacher... take too long. He want it fast."
On screen: 📚⚡

ORDEAL (14-38 s)
"So one day, a very, very old man come to the river.
He take sand. One handful. Throw in the river. Another handful. Throw.
The young man laugh. 'What you doing, old man?'
'I make a bridge. So people can walk across.'
'With sand? You cannot stop a river with sand!'
(pause)
The old man look at him. 'Yes. Same as you. Your fast way... cannot work. Go learn from a teacher.'
The old man was the king of the gods. In disguise."
(1 s)
"Like us. We want strong... fast. So we buy. Ice. Massage gun. Magic drink."

TAKEAWAY (38-54 s)
"In Ashtanga, every practice end the same. Lie down. Savasana. Take rest.
Saturday... rest day. No practice.
Nobody can sell it to you. You have to take it.
Savasana look easy. For busy people... it's the hardest asana. Do nothing. Let the body build."
[Boonchu cue: one line in your own words on why students skip Savasana or rush the rest day. Replace a line above if you have it.]

RETURN (54-60 s)
(silent beat, 1 s)
"What scientists say really work? Sleep. Good food. Rest day.
Recovery... cannot be bottled and sold."
On screen: 😴 sleep · 🍚 food · 📅 rest day ✅

Payoff check: the hook asks "what really works?"; the last line answers it with the article's own words. One ordeal (wanting the fast way), one lesson (the slow, simple way works; rest is taken, not bought). The story stops before Yavakri's ending on purpose (see sources).

Edit cues (for the editing session): zoom punch on "careful", "sand", "river", "cannot work", "take rest", "cannot be bottled"; SFX pop 0.4 s after each, a soft splash under each handful of sand, a tiny ice clink under "ice bath"; cards: (1) felt bucket of ice cubes with a worried felt muscle peeking out; (2) the old man throwing sand into the river while the young man laughs; (3) a Savasana photo from class (no student name, face not clear) or Boonchu in Savasana.
Picture prompt (2): "stop-motion puppet animation film still in the style of Wes Anderson's Isle of Dogs: a handmade tabletop diorama, a very old thin felt puppet sage with a long white wool beard and glassy bead eyes kneeling at a river of cut-felt blue waves, tossing a small handful of sand-colored felt crumbs into the water, a young felt puppet in a saffron cloth standing on the bank laughing, a tiny pile of sand at the river edge, painted cardboard sky, cotton-wool clouds on visible wires, flat front-on camera, centered symmetrical tableau, visible fibers and handmade imperfections, no text"
Picture prompt (1): "stop-motion puppet animation film still in the style of Wes Anderson's Isle of Dogs: a handmade tabletop diorama, a small wooden tub full of clear resin ice cubes, a tired felt puppet sitting in it shivering with a wool towel on its head, a felt massage gun and a bright bottle of drink on a little cardboard shelf beside it, painted cardboard wall, flat front-on camera, centered symmetrical tableau, visible fibers and handmade imperfections, no text"

## Sources (check before filming)
- News: ScienceDaily, 14 Oct 2026, "Scientists reveal why some workout recovery tricks can backfire" (The Conversation; Max Davis, University of Bath): https://www.sciencedaily.com/releases/2026/10/261013015805.htm
- Story: Mahabharata, Vana Parva (Tirtha-yatra), Section 135, Ganguli translation: https://neonvagabond.xyz/mirrors/sacred-texts/hin/m03/m03135.htm (mirror of sacred-texts.com/hin/m03/m03135.htm). Checked word for word:
  - Yavakri, son of Bharadwaja, wants the Vedas fast: "a knowledge of the Vedas as learnt through teachers, is acquired in a long time".
  - Indra: "the way that thou hast adopted is not the proper way... Go and learn from the lips of a preceptor."
  - Indra as "an ascetic Brahmana, hundreds of years old, and infirm" throws "handfuls of sand into the Bhagirathi" (the Ganga) to make a dam "so that there may be a commodious passage".
  - Yavakri laughs: "thou canst not dam up this mighty current... desist from what is impracticable."
  - Indra: "even as, for obtaining a knowledge of the Vedas, thou hast begun these penances, which can never be fruitful."
- Left out on purpose: after this, Indra still gives Yavakri the boon, his father warns him about pride, and later he dies. The script stops at the dam and says nothing about the ending. The script line joins two true lines from the same chapter: "can never be fruitful" (at the river) and "go and learn from the lips of a preceptor" (Indra's first visit). The captions shorten it to "you can't skip the teacher".
- Ashtanga facts used: Saturday rest day, every practice ends lying down ("take rest"). No numbers, no medical claims.
- Not the same as yesterday's science: no bird study, no tortoise. Bharadvajasana (29 Sep) is the father's asana; this story is the son's and the asana is Savasana, not used in 30 days.

## 2026-10-15 · plato-tortoise-mountain

# Plato · 15 Oct 2026 · daily Reel package
Hidden Stories of Ashtanga (no Ep. number until filmed; last posted Ep. 4)
"The Tortoise Who Carried a Mountain"

Route: Lane A (Hidden Stories). Last 3 pieces: Bhagiratha script 14 Oct (Lane A, not filmed), Kumbhakarna script 13 Oct (Lane A, not filmed), Mysore Reel set for Fri 16 Oct (Lane B). Next real post is Lane B, so this one is on route.
Habit: #2 Get stronger (body-weight strength: arm balances) + the core habit (same practice, every day).

Trigger note: the only unused life note (13 Oct, the hypermobility jab) still needs his own words, so not used. No @Plato request in the team chat.
News picked: Penn State study, Proceedings of the Royal Society B, on ScienceDaily 14 Oct 2026. 12 guinea fowl (a bird like a chicken). 6 young birds wore a small lead band on one leg (4% of body weight) from week 2 to week 16. 6 birds wore nothing. Then all walked on a treadmill with the weight. The birds with no training used 23% more energy. The trained birds used no extra energy at all. The authors say: in humans, not tested yet.
Asana note: Kurmasana (the tortoise asana) would be the perfect name match, but Supta Kurmasana is the Ep. 5 script (3 Oct, Foolish Old Man), so it is inside 30 days. I used Bhujapidasana, the asana just before it. If you want the tortoise name, swap to Kurmasana and move Ep. 5 later.

## The link (4 lines, told to a 10-year-old)
- Trigger: baby birds wore a tiny weight on one leg, every day. When they grew up, the weight cost them nothing.
- Story: the gods want a magic drink, so they stir the whole ocean with a mountain. The mountain sinks. A big tortoise goes under and holds it on his back. Everybody else gets tired. One old book says the tortoise felt it like a back scratch (Mahabharata, Adi Parva 18; Bhagavata Purana 8.7.10).
- Behavior: heavy does not get lighter. You get used to carrying it, a little, every day.
- Asana: Bhujapidasana, "arm pressure". Your arms carry your whole body. Week one: a mountain. Every morning a little. One day: a back scratch.

Point in one line a child can repeat: "Same weight. The one who carries it every day doesn't get tired."

Toolkit pick: Question-First (two groups, same weight, only one gets tired: why?) + /story 4 beats with fail, hero, payoff. Fail = everybody who stirs and gets tired (and you in week one). Hero = the tortoise, then the viewer on the mat. Payoff = the birds' answer, held to the end. Why: the question is pure curiosity with no science words, it works sound-off, and the answer (every day since small) is the lesson itself, so the reveal and the takeaway land together. Last two days were Curiosity Amplifiers and Name-the-Feeling style, so this rotates.
Other options: (1) Win/Loss Frame: "Untrained birds: 23% more tired. Trained birds: zero." then the tortoise. (2) Pattern interrupt: Boonchu ties a small bag to his own ankle on camera, "Baby birds wear this. Every day. 14 weeks."

## Hooks (/hook, answered myself)
Topic: what you carry a little every day stops being heavy.
Consensus: "Heavy things are heavy. Some people are just strong."
Where Boonchu sharpens it: 20 years of the same practice, six days a week since 2008. He has seen arm balances go from shaking to quiet by coming back, not by being strong on day one.
1. Authority: "Scientists tie a little weight on baby birds. Every day. Fourteen weeks."
2. Contrarian: "Heavy never get lighter. Ask a bird."
3. Stakes: "Two birds. Same weight. One get tired. One... nothing."
4. Question: "Same weight on the leg. Only one group get tired. Why?"
5. Pattern interrupt: (he ties a small bag to his ankle) "This... every day. Since baby."
Pick: 4 at 0-3 s (on screen: "Same weight. Only one got tired. Why?"), then 3's picture as the second hook at 3-6 s. Why: it opens a loop with no answer given, a 10-year-old gets it with the sound off, and the answer waits to the end (REVEAL AT THE END).
Cover: "Same weight. Why?" with "Why?" in gold. 3 words, passes the black and white check.

## Script (/story, 60 s, spoken to camera, his spoken English)
HOOK (0-6 s)
"Scientists take two group of birds. Same little weight on the leg."
(pause) "One group... very tired. The other group... nothing. Not tired at all."
"Why? Wait. First, a tortoise."
On screen: 🐦 tired 😩 · 🐦 not tired 😌 · ❓

CONTEXT (6-15 s)
"Bedtime story. Long time ago, the gods want a magic drink.
To make it... they must stir the whole ocean. With a mountain. Like a spoon."
On screen: 🏔️🥄🌊

ORDEAL (15-38 s)
"But the mountain is too heavy. It sink.
So a big tortoise swim under the sea... and hold the mountain on his back.
Everybody stir and stir. Pull, pull, pull.
And they get tired. They say: 'We have no strength left.'
(pause)
The tortoise? Still holding.
One old book say... the mountain turning on his back... feel like a nice back scratch."
(smile, 1 s)
"Like you. First week on the mat. Every arm balance... a mountain."

TAKEAWAY (38-54 s)
"In Ashtanga we have Bhujapidasana. Bhuja mean arm.
Your arms carry your whole body.
First week... shaking. Heavy. Okay.
Every morning, a little bit. Same arms. Same weight.
The weight don't get lighter. You get stronger."
[Boonchu cue: one line in your own words on how you teach Bhujapidasana, e.g. where people put the weight. Replace the line above if you have it.]

RETURN (54-60 s)
(silent beat, 1 s)
"The birds who don't get tired? They carry the weight every day... since they were baby."
On screen: every day since small ✅ · "Birds only. People: not tested yet."

Payoff check: the hook asks "why only one group?"; the last line answers it. One ordeal (carry it every day), one lesson. The science limit is on screen, so nobody says we claimed it works in people.

Edit cues (for the editing session): zoom punch on "same weight", "nothing", "mountain", "back scratch", "every day"; SFX pop 0.4 s after each, thunder-bass under "it sink"; cards: (1) two felt birds on a felt treadmill, one sweating, one calm; (2) felt mountain spinning on a puppet tortoise's back, a rope of wool around it; (3) Bhujapidasana photo from class (no student name).

## Sources (check before filming)
- News: ScienceDaily, 14 Oct 2026, "The secret to easier exercise as an adult may start in childhood" (Penn State; Proc. R. Soc. B 293, issue 2074): https://www.sciencedaily.com/releases/2026/10/261013015811.htm . 12 birds, 6 per group. Untrained birds +23% energy with the load; trained birds no extra. "Whether the same adaptations occur in humans remains to be established."
- Story: Mahabharata, Adi Parva (Astika Parva) Section 18, Ganguli translation (Project Gutenberg #15474): the gods ask the tortoise-king to hold the mountain on his back; later "we are spent up, we have no strength left to churn further". ## The link (4 lines, told to a 10-year-old)
- Trigger: a runner came 3rd. Next year, 2nd. This year, 1st. He did not give up after 3rd.
- Story: a king, Bhagiratha, wants to bring the river Ganga down from the sky. His grandfather tried. Could not. His father tried. Could not. Bhagiratha tries. Even after the god says yes, he must stand on one big toe for a whole year. Then Shiva catches the river in his hair, and it comes down (Valmiki Ramayana, Bala Kanda 42 and 43).
- Behavior: a try that does not win is not a fail. It is a step. Somebody keeps going.
- Asana: Padangusthasana, "big toe asana". Today the hands do not reach the toes. Next month, closer. You come back every morning.

Point in one line a child can repeat: "Third. Second. First. Every try is a step."

Toolkit pick: Curiosity Amplifiers (specific numbers, a ladder: 3rd, 2nd, 1st) as the hook + /story 4 beats with fail, hero, payoff. Fail = grandfather and father (not bad people, just not yet). Hero = Bhagiratha, then the viewer on the mat. Payoff = closes the 3rd, 2nd, 1st loop. Why: the 3-2-1 ladder is plain enough for a 10-year-old with no sport knowledge, and the same ladder sits inside the myth (grandfather, father, son), so news and myth click with no explaining.
Other options: (1) Name-the-Feeling: "You try. You don't get it. You think: I'm not made for this." then the king. (2) Fork-First: "One more year on one toe... or go home? What would you do?" (stop at the hard part, then show).

## Hooks (/hook, answered myself)
Topic: a try that does not win is a step, not a fail.
Consensus: "If you didn't get it, you failed. Try something else."
Where Boonchu sharpens it: 20 years of the same practice, six days a week since 2008. Asanas come in steps over years, never in one big day. In the Mysore room you come back, and the teacher sees the steps you can't.
1. Authority: "Twenty years, same practice. Nobody gets it on the first try."
2. Contrarian: "Third place is not a fail. Ask this runner."
3. Stakes: "If you stop at third... you never see first."
4. Question: "Third place. Then second. What happen next year?"
5. Pattern interrupt: "This king stood on one toe. For one whole year."
Pick: 4 as the 0-3 s curiosity (on screen: "3rd. 2nd. ..." with the third box empty, sound-off works), then 5 as the second hook at 3-6 s, to jump into the myth. Why: the empty "1st" box opens a loop everyone wants closed, and the toe line is a strange picture that keeps them past 6 s.
Cover: "Third. Second. ..." with "..." in gold. 3 words, passes the black and white check.

## Script (/story, 60 s, spoken to camera, his spoken English)
HOOK (0-6 s)
"One runner. Chicago Marathon. Two year ago, he come third. Last year... second."
(pause) "This week?"
(beat, no answer yet) "Wait. First, a king who stand on one toe. For one year."
On screen: 3rd ✅ 2nd ✅ 1st ❓

CONTEXT (6-15 s)
"In the Ramayana, a king want to bring the river Ganga down from the sky.
His grandfather try. Very hard. Cannot.
His father try. Cannot."
On screen: grandfather ❌ father ❌

ORDEAL (15-40 s)
"Then the son, Bhagiratha, he try.
Many many years. Finally the god say: okay.
But... the river is too strong. It will break the earth.
Only Shiva can catch it.
So Bhagiratha stand on one big toe. One whole year. To ask Shiva.
(pause)
Shiva say yes. The river fall from the sky... into Shiva's hair. And then, slowly... it come down to the people."
(silent beat, 1 s)
"His grandfather was not a failure. His father was not a failure. They were the steps."

TAKEAWAY (40-55 s)
"In Ashtanga we have Padangusthasana. Padangustha mean... big toe.
First day, your hands don't reach your toe. Okay.
Next month, a little closer. Next year, closer.
That is not fail. That is third... and second."

RETURN (55-60 s)
"And the runner? This week... first place.
He say: 'I was ready.'"
On screen: 1st ✅

Payoff check: the hook opens "what happen next year?"; the last line closes it with 1st and his own words. One ordeal (keep going after "not yet"), one lesson.

Edit cues (for the editing session): zoom punch on "third", "second", "one toe", "first"; SFX pop 0.4 s after each; cards: (1) 3-2-1 podium boxes filling in, (2) Isle of Dogs puppet king on one big toe on a felt mountain, (3) felt river falling into a wool-hair Shiva seen from behind (no face needed), (4) Boonchu folding in Padangusthasana.

## 2026-10-13 · plato-sleeping-giant

# Plato · 13 Oct 2026 · daily Reel package
Hidden Stories of Ashtanga (no Ep. number until filmed; last posted Ep. 4)
"The Giant Who Did Everything in One Day"

Route: Lane A story with a Lane B door (EN/TH caption says beginners welcome). Chinese stays Lane A (no promo).
Habit: #4 Sleep (careful words: "scientists saw", never "Ashtanga fixes sleep") + the core (same practice, same time, a teacher who notices).

Trigger note: no unused life note, no @Plato request. News checked 10 to 13 Oct (ScienceDaily fitness). Picked: ScienceDaily 11 Oct 2026, University of Texas at Austin (Corral, Baird; Journal of Physical Activity & Health). Young adults, mostly students, wore Fitbits for months. People who moved on MORE DAYS got more deep sleep. As little as 10 minutes a day of brisk activity was linked to better sleep, and deep sleep was linked to better mood the next day. Quote (Corral): "daily movement may be better for sleep than doing it all on the weekend." Limits: it only watched people (it does not prove cause), young adults only, and it did NOT test daily vs the same total on fewer days. The script says "scientists saw", never "proved". Left out: fat-cell injection (11 Oct, a drug), Menin protein (12 Oct, mice, too technical), knee study (used yesterday).

## The link (4 lines, told to a 10-year-old)
- Trigger: scientists put a watch on young people for months. The ones who moved a little, on more days, slept deeper. Even 10 minutes a day.
- Story: Kumbhakarna is a giant who is always hungry. Brahma, the god who makes the world, says: you sleep. His brother begs. So: sleep six months, wake up one day (Valmiki Ramayana, Yuddha Kanda 61.28).
- Behavior: everything in one day, then nothing for a long time. That is the giant's way. The body likes a little, every day.
- Asana: Yoganidrasana, "yoga sleep". Nobody gets it in one big day. A little, every morning.

Point in one line a child can repeat: "Don't be the giant. A little, every day."

Toolkit pick: Chris Chung #3 (common belief, then contradict: "exercise hard on the weekend, you sleep well") as the hook + Boonchu's 5-beat order (curiosity, second hook, setup, payoff, punch) + fail-hero-payoff. Fail = the giant (everything in one day); Hero = the viewer on the mat, a little every morning; Payoff = closes the sleep hook. Why: sleep is a feeling everyone has (tired, bad nights), "weekend" is the viewer's own week (mirror), and the giant is a funny, safe, kid-friendly picture. Last 3 picks: Curiosity Amplifiers (12), pattern interrupt + Fork-First (11), Triple Hook (10).
Other options: (1) Name-the-Feeling: "You go to bed tired. You wake up tired." then the study. (2) Gervais open on himself: "I am in my fifties. I sleep like a baby. I also wake up at 4 like a baby." (needs his OK that it is true).

## Hooks (/hook, answered myself)
Topic: a little every day beats everything on one day (sleep study + the sleeping giant).
Consensus: "Train hard on the weekend, you will sleep well."
Where Boonchu sharpens it: Ashtanga is built as a daily habit (six mornings a week), and in the Mysore room the teacher notices when you are gone. Small, daily, same time.
1. Authority: "Scientists watched young people sleep for months. The deep sleepers had one thing in common."
2. Contrarian: "Two hours of exercise on Saturday? Maybe not the best for your sleep."
3. Stakes: "You can exercise every weekend... and still sleep like you never did."
4. Question: "Who sleeps deeper: the weekend warrior, or the ten-minutes-a-day person?"
5. Pattern interrupt: "Ten minutes. Every day. Scientists say your sleep likes this more."
Pick: 4 as the 0-3 s curiosity (opens a loop, sound-off works as 2 lines on screen), then 1 as the second hook, with the giant as the twist. Why: a question with two people in it lets the viewer pick a side (they are usually the weekend one: mirror), and the answer is held to the payoff. Cover: "Don't be the giant." with "giant" in gold.

## Script (60 s, spoken to camera, his spoken English, Boonchu's 5-beat order)
1 CURIOSITY (0-3 s)
"Who sleep deeper?
Big exercise on Saturday... or ten minutes, every day?"
[on screen: 🏋️ "Saturday: 2 hours" vs ⏱️ "Every day: 10 minutes"] [zoom punch: "deeper"]

2 SECOND HOOK (3-8 s)
"Scientists put a watch on young people. For months.
And the answer... a giant in the Ramayana already know it."
[card 1: felt wrist with a tiny watch, a sleeping felt puppet] [zoom punch: "giant"]

3 SETUP: THE STORY, bedtime voice (8-32 s)
"At night I tell my son this story.
There is a giant. Kumbhakarna. Very big. Always hungry.
The gods are worried.
So Brahma, the god who make the world, say: 'You... sleep.'
His brother say: 'Please. Let him wake up sometimes.'
So Brahma say: 'Okay. Sleep six months. Wake up one day.'"
[silent beat, 1 s]
"One day!
So in one day... he do everything.
Eat a mountain of food. Walk. Shout.
Then... boom. Sleep again. Six months."
[SFX: snore on "boom"]
MIRROR: "Maybe you know this giant.
Monday to Friday... nothing.
Saturday... two hours, everything.
Sunday... everything hurt."

4 PAYOFF (32-52 s)
"The scientists see this:
people who move on more days... more deep sleep.
Even ten minutes a day.
Body don't like the giant way.
Body like a little... every day.
In Ashtanga we practise six mornings a week. Same time. Same room.
And if you don't come... we notice."
[card: ❌ "everything in one day" / ✅ "a little, every day"]
"We also have a sleep asana. Yoganidrasana. Yoga sleep.
[BOONCHU'S OWN CUE GOES HERE. Plato's draft only: 'Lie on your back. One leg behind the head, then the other. Hands hold behind your back.']
Nobody get it in one big day. A little... every morning."

5 PUNCH (52-60 s)
"It is called yoga sleep.
(pause)
Nobody ever fall asleep in it."

Payoff check: the hook asks "who sleeps deeper?"; the payoff answers it (a little, on more days) before the punch. One lesson only: a little every day beats everything in one day. Not told: why the gods worried in detail (he ate living beings: too dark), the war, his death, Ravana's name, Saraswati and the tongue mix-up (a later Uttara Kanda telling, not checked today).

Verify before filming:
- His own Yoganidrasana cue (the one above is Plato's draft). Also: is the punch true for him? If a student ever did fall asleep in it, swap the punch for that real story.
- "Six mornings a week, same time, same room": Ashtanga tradition is six days. Check it matches the AYBKK timetable he wants to show.
- Study words: "scientists see", "more deep sleep", "even ten minutes". It is an observation study of young adults with Fitbits. Never say Ashtanga or yoga improves sleep. Never say "two hours on Saturday is bad": the study did not test that. The Saturday line is the giant's picture and the viewer's own week, not a finding.
- Story check: Valmiki Ramayana, Yuddha Kanda 61.24-28 (K.M.K. Murthy translation on valmikiramayan.net): Brahma curses him to sleep; Ravana, his brother, says "let some space of time be fixed for his sleeping and waking"; Brahma: "He will indeed sleep for six months and wake up for a day" (61.28). "Mountain of food": in Yuddha Kanda 60 the rakshasas pile up heaps of food to wake him; "mountain" is our kid word for "heaps". "Walk, shout" is our plain picture (61.29 says he wanders the earth; the text says he eats people, left out).
- Thailand: Kumbhakarna = Kumphakan (กุมภกรรณ) in the Ramakien, a well-loved character. Keep it soft and funny about sleep and food only. No fight, no battle with Rama.

## Edit cue sheet (for the edit session)
- Header pill: "Hidden Stories of Ashtanga · Ep. N" (N = last posted + 1 when filmed).
- Zoom punches 1.3x on: "deeper", "giant", "Kumbhakarna", "hungry", "sleep", "six months", "one day", "everything", "boom", "nothing", "hurt", "more days", "ten minutes", "notice", "Yoganidrasana", last "asleep". SFX at 40%, 0.4 s after the word: chime (deeper), impact-bass-1 (giant), pop (each item "eat / walk / shout"), synth snore or impact-bass-2 (boom), error (hurt), ping (ten minutes), sparkle (notice), click-soft before the punch, no SFX on the last line (silence, then cut).
- Week card: "Mon · Tue · Wed · Thu · Fri" grey with 💤, "Sat" red 🔥, "Sun" 🤕, as he says them.
- Card list reveal: ❌ "everything in one day" then ✅ "a little, every day".
- Captions: Weight Shift, big emoji: 😴 ⌚ 👹 🍚 💤 🔥 🤕 🧘
- Cards (Isle of Dogs stop-motion prompts, handmade cues kept):
  1. "stop-motion puppet animation film still in the style of Wes Anderson's Isle of Dogs: a handmade tabletop diorama, a felt puppet asleep in a tiny knitted bed, a little felt wristwatch on its arm glowing softly, painted cardboard night sky, cotton-wool clouds on visible wires, a paper moon, flat front-on camera, centered symmetrical tableau, visible fibers and handmade imperfections"

阿斯汤加的隐藏故事 😴
周末狂练两小时，和每天动十分钟，谁睡得更香？
美国的科学家让一群年轻人戴着手环，看了好几个月：一周里动的天数越多，深度睡眠越多。哪怕每天只动十分钟。
《罗摩衍那》里有个巨人，叫昆巴卡尔纳。👹
他太能吃了，众神都发愁。梵天说：你去睡吧。他哥哥求情，梵天就定了规矩：睡六个月，醒一天。
只有一天，他就把所有事都塞进这一天：吃掉像山一样多的饭，到处走，大声喊……然后“咚”，又睡六个月。💤
阿斯汤加里有个体式叫“瑜伽睡眠式”（Yoganidrasana）。没有人能一天练成，只能每天早上，一点一点来。🧘
（这项研究只是观察，还不能证明原因。）

## Caption EN
Breath three. Everything shakes. Many people stop breathing here and do not know it.
You want five breaths to feel like five.
Led primary, Monday 12 Oct, AYBKK. Lights on at 4:30 AM. Every mat wet. Only the one counting stayed dry.
DM me MYSORE for your first Mysore class guide. In the same chat, tell me:
When was the last time you almost quit?

## 2026-10-12 · plato-monkey-bridge

# Plato · 12 Oct 2026 · daily Reel package
Hidden Stories of Ashtanga (no Ep. number until filmed; last posted Ep. 4)
"The Monkeys Who Built a Straight Bridge"

Route: Lane A story with a Lane B door (EN/TH caption says beginners welcome). Chinese stays Lane A (no promo). The men's hook and the Lane B fear buster are still waiting in chat for the Lane B slot.
Habit: the core (a teacher who notices when you go off the line) + #8 Mind-body movement.

Trigger note: no unused life note, no @Plato request. News checked 9 to 12 Oct. Picked: ScienceDaily 10 Oct 2026 on a knee study (Lancet Rheumatology trial, University of Utah with Stanford and NYU). 68 people with sore knees (mild to moderate knee arthritis, inner side). Scientists measured how each one walks and picked a tiny turn of the foot (5 or 10 degrees in or out) just for that person. A small buzz on the shin told them when the foot went back to the old way. One year later, their pain went down 2.5 points (0 to 10 scale) vs 1.3 in the group who kept their old walk. Before this, trials gave everyone the same fix, and for some people it did nothing or made the knee load worse. Left out: "Only One Workout Helped Older Adults Lose Fat" (9 Oct, 2025 paper, small effects, and it is a HIIT claim we must not tie to Ashtanga).

## The link (4 lines, told to a 10-year-old)
- Trigger: some people had sore knees. They walked every day, still sore. Scientists turned their foot a tiny bit, a different turn for each person. One year later: much less pain.
- Story: Rama has to cross the sea. No boat. Strong monkeys throw big rocks and trees into the water. Nala, the builder, leads the work. Some monkeys pull a long string, some measure with sticks. In five days the bridge reaches the other side (Valmiki Ramayana, Yuddha Kanda 22).
- Behavior: strong arms bring the rock. The line puts it in the right place.
- Asana: Setu Bandhasana (setu = bridge). Your feet and your head must be in the right place before you lift. Same work, on the right line.

Point in one line a child can repeat: "Strong brings the rock. The line builds the bridge."

Toolkit pick: Curiosity Amplifiers hook (specific + contrast: "walk every day, still sore... a tiny turn") + /story beats, fail-hero-payoff. Fail = the old way (same fix for everyone, or just "try harder"); Hero = Nala's line, and the measured turn; Payoff = the last line closes the sore-knee hook. Why: the hook is a plain everyday thing (sore knee, walking), no sport words, and "tiny" is a surprise that holds the viewer. Last 3 picks were /hook pattern interrupt + Fork-First (11), Triple Hook (10), Myth-Kill (9).
Other options: (1) Name-the-Feeling: "You practise every day. Hard. And you are stuck in the same place." then the story. (2) Gervais close on himself: "Twenty years, I thought I needed more strength. I needed a string." (needs his OK that it is true).

## Hooks (/hook, answered myself)
Topic: hard work on the wrong line (a "stuck" asana), via the knee study.
Consensus: "Stuck? Practise harder. Get stronger."
Where Boonchu sharpens it: the fix is often a small change of place (where the foot, hand or head goes), measured for YOUR body, then done every day. His own words (Supta Kurmasana, 4 Oct): "it's like you scratch where you don't itch."
1. Authority: "Scientists watched sore knees for one year. The answer was a tiny turn of the foot."
2. Contrarian: "Sore knees didn't need more walking. They needed a better line."
3. Stakes: "You can work hard every day on the wrong line. For years."
4. Question: "Why do some people practise every day and stay stuck?"
5. Pattern interrupt: "Turn your foot. Just a tiny bit. Scientists did this for one year."
Pick: a mix of 1 and 5, opening with the trigger in kid words: "Some people have sore knees. They walk every day. Still sore. Then scientists turn their foot... just a tiny bit." Why: a 10-year-old gets it, it works with sound off as 3 short lines, and "tiny" sets the trap the story snaps (not stronger, just the right place). Cover: "Not stronger. Straighter." with "Straighter" in gold.

## Script (60 s, spoken to camera, his spoken English)
HOOK (0-6 s)
"Some people have sore knee. They walk every day. Still sore.
Then scientists turn their foot... just a tiny bit."
[zoom punch: "sore" / "tiny"] [card 1: felt foot turning on a felt floor]

CONTEXT + MIRROR (6-16 s)
"Before, everybody get the same fix. For some people... nothing.
This time, they measure each person. A tiny turn, only for you.
And a small buzz on the leg... when the foot go back to the old way.
One year later... much less pain.
Maybe you are like this. You practise every day. Hard. Still stuck."
[card: ❌ "same fix for everybody" / ✅ "a tiny turn, just for you"]

ORDEAL: THE STORY, bedtime voice (16-42 s)
"At night I tell my son a story. From the Ramayana.
Rama must cross the sea. No boat. Big, big sea.
The monkeys are so strong. They pull rocks... big like elephants. Whole trees.
Throw, throw, into the sea.
One monkey, Nala, is the builder. His father build houses for the gods.
So some monkeys pull a long, long string.
Some measure with sticks.
Every rock go on the line.
Day one, a little. Day two, more. Day five...
they reach the other side."
[silent beat, 2 s, soft wave sound]
"Strong bring the rock.
The line build the bridge."

TAKEAWAY: THE ASANA (42-54 s)
"In Ashtanga, we have Setu Bandhasana. Setu mean bridge.
[BOONCHU'S OWN CUE GOES HERE. Plato's draft only: 'Heels together. Toes turn out. Top of the head on the floor. Then slowly lift the hips.']
Not more strong. The right place first.
In our Mysore room, the teacher is the string.
When you go off the line... we see it."

PAYOFF (54-60 s)
"Same walk. Every day. Just a tiny turn.
Strong bring the rock. The line build the bridge."

Payoff check: the hook opens "walk every day, still sore"; the end closes it ("same walk, every day, just a tiny turn"). One lesson only: hard work needs the right line. Not taught: the war, Ravana, the ocean god, the 5 numbers of the days.

Verify before filming:
- His own Setu Bandhasana cue (the one above is Plato's draft). Is "heels together, toes out" how he teaches it? It also ties to the knee study (foot angle) if true.
- Setu Bandhasana loads the neck. Boonchu may want a line like "go slow, your teacher shows you": his call.
- "Much less pain" = 2.5 vs 1.3 points on a 0 to 10 scale after one year. Spoken line says "much less pain" only, no number. Never say yoga or Ashtanga fixes knees. Never tell viewers to turn their feet: the scientists measured each person in a lab. Caption says this.
- The strings: Valmiki 6.22.60 says some monkeys "drew up strings a hundred Yojanas long", 6.22.62 some held "poles for measuring". The text does not say WHY in a quote; "so every rock goes on the line" is our plain reading. If he wants zero reading-in, say: "Some monkeys pull a long string. Some measure with sticks." and stop.
- No invented dialogue: Nala's speech in the book is about his own skill ("I am capable of constructing a bridge"), not about strings. The script gives him no quote.
- In Thailand this is a Ramakien favourite (Nilaphat / Nala). Keep it Valmiki, keep it soft: no fight, no Lanka battle, no Ravana.

## Edit cue sheet (for the edit session)
- Header pill: "Hidden Stories of Ashtanga · Ep. N" (N = last posted + 1 when filmed).
- Zoom punches 1.3x on: "sore", "tiny", "nothing", "only for you", "stuck", "elephants", "Nala", "string", "line", "other side", "bridge", "Setu", "we see it". SFX at 40%, 0.4 s after the word: click-soft (tiny), error (nothing), impact-bass-1 (elephants, each "throw"), ping (string), chime (line), whoosh (other side), sparkle (bridge), impact-bass-2 (last "bridge"). Soft wave sound under the silent beat.
- Card list reveal: ❌ "same fix for everybody" then ✅ "a tiny turn, just for you".
- Day counter card: "Day 1 · Day 2 · Day 3 · Day 4 · Day 5" filling in as he counts (no yojana numbers).
- Captions: Weight Shift, big emoji: 🦵 🦶 🐒 🪨 🧵 🌉 🧘

## Sources (check before filming)
- News: ScienceDaily, 10 Oct 2026, "A Surprisingly Simple Way to Relieve Knee Arthritis Pain Without Pills or Surgery": https://www.sciencedaily.com/releases/2026/10/261009025434.htm
  Study: Uhlrich et al., "Personalised gait retraining for medial compartment knee osteoarthritis: a randomised controlled trial", The Lancet Rheumatology 2025; 7(10): e708, DOI 10.1016/S2665-9913(25)00151-1. 68 people, 34 per group, 6 weekly sessions, then at least 20 min a day at home, buzz feedback on the shin, 1 year. Pain down 2.5 vs 1.3 points (0 to 10). Limits: small, mild to moderate inner-knee arthritis only; the paper is from 2025, the news story is from this week.
- Story: Valmiki Ramayana, Yuddha Kanda, sarga 22 (K.M.K. Murthy translation): https://www.valmikiramayan.net/utf8/yuddha/sarga22/yuddhasans22.htm
  6.22.44 Nala, "the son of Vishvakarma"; 6.22.53 and 58 the monkeys break and drag rocks and trees, "elephant-sized rocks"; 6.22.60 "Some others drew up strings a hundred Yojanas long"; 6.22.62 "holding poles for measuring the bridge"; 6.22.66-70 the bridge grows each day for 5 days and reaches the far shore.
  Left out on purpose: why Rama crosses (the war), the ocean god, the yojana numbers. The floating stones and the squirrel are NOT in Valmiki (later tellings), so they are not used.
- Asana: Setu Bandhasana (setu = bridge, bandha = lock or build), the last asana of the Ashtanga Primary series.

## Option 1 (Plato's pick): Eyes Covered
Toolkit: Fork-First Story + /story 4 beats.
Hook (to his son): "Close your eyes. Okay... now you are far from home. No people. Which way?" / "An old Indian father tells his son this story. Tonight... I tell mine." On screen: "Eyes covered. Far from home. Which way?"
Body: Uddalaka tells his son Shvetaketu (Chandogya Upanishad 6.14, tr. F. Max Muller 1879). FAIL: a man's eyes are covered, he is left where no people live, turns east, north, west and shouts. HERO: someone (no name) takes the cover off and points: "Go that way. That is Gandhara." PAYOFF: he still asks the way village to village and walks home himself. Adults: Mysore style, the teacher points to the next asana, you walk it, morning after morning. [Boonchu's cue] in the payoff only. Last line: "Another old yoga text says: Gu is darkness. Ru takes it away. It never says Ru walks for you."
Facts: Muller wording only (east, north, west; "a place where there are no human beings"; "arrive at last at Gandhara"). No robbers/forest/tigers (later commentary). Helper unnamed. Never "Gandhara = Kandahar". Gu-Ru = Advayataraka Upanishad 16, tr. T. R. Srinivasa Ayyangar 1938, p. 8; present as what the text says (dictionary root of guru = heavy, Monier-Williams p. 359; gu/ru is a folk etymology). Do not quote verses 17-18 (self-glorifying). Never say "ancient" or a date.

## Option 2: Lights Off
Toolkit: Name-the-Feeling + Hero's Journey. Dropback into Urdhva Dhanurasana, you cannot see behind you, teacher's hands until you don't need them. Weak: no myth; says "Gu" in the first 3 s (breaks REVEAL AT THE END); repeats the 7 Oct 'Hand That Knew the Way' (dark + dropback).

## Option 3: Guru Means Heavy
Toolkit: Root and Wobble + Vibe Samurai, Gervais aimed at himself. Dictionary: guru = heavy (gravis, barys). Screenshot line: "Light shows you the floor. Weight keeps you on it." Asana: Samasthitih. Weak: no bedtime story; argues with his own carousel line on the same day; can read as mocking a devotional idea.

- Shot: top-down desk at first light (laptop, phone, planner); his hand slides them aside, lifts a rolled mat; cut to him at the shala door, mat under arm, one tap on 'under my arm'. No clock times.

## Same ad in Boonchu's WRONG FIX recipe (he asked: 'If you want to be successful... this is a hook recipe, right?')
Plato: yes, it is his recipe, but 'If you want to be successful' skips the 'and have this' part (a wish, not a problem), so nobody feels called out. Fill step 1 with a situation people see in themselves (actions, not health/mood attributes, for Meta).
A (work): If you work hard every day... and still don't see big results... working harder is not going to fix it alone. But one small win every morning, on your mat... that builds the best of you.
B (morning): If you start your day with your phone and emails... more coffee is not going to fix it alone. But take the sunrise on your mat... and your day starts with a win.
C (hours): If you want success... and you already work long hours... more hours is not the answer. But one small success every morning... it builds up.

## 2026-10-11 · plato-thirsty-crane

# Plato · 11 Oct 2026 · daily Reel package
Hidden Stories of Ashtanga (no Ep. number until filmed; last posted Ep. 4)
"The Brothers Who Were Too Thirsty"

Route: Lane A story with a Lane B door (Bakasana is an arm balance a beginner can start working on; the EN/TH caption says beginners welcome). Last 3 daily scripts were Lane A and not filmed (frogs, Aruna, Upamanyu rewrite). Next due is Lane B: the men's hook and the Lane B fear buster are already waiting in chat, so this one stays Lane A. Chinese stays Lane A (no promo).
Habit: #2 Get stronger (arm balances) + #8 Mind-body movement.

Trigger note: no unused life note, no @Plato request. News checked 8 to 10 Oct (ScienceDaily health): vaping to quit smoking (off-limits: e-cigarettes are banned in Thailand), vitamin D2 vs D3 (needs outside knowledge, fails PLAIN TRIGGER), loneliness study (strong, but it repeats the doves Reel of 6 Oct and the Upamanyu rewrite waiting in chat; kept for later, see the end). So, per the PLAIN TRIGGER order, the trigger is the viewer's own body: you rush into an arm balance and your nose hits the mat.

## The link (4 lines)
- Trigger: you put your hands down, you jump, your nose hits the floor.
- Story: five brothers lost in the forest, very thirsty. At a clear lake a crane's voice says "answer my questions first, then drink". Four brothers drink anyway and fall down. The fifth stops, listens, answers, and all four wake up "as if... from slumber" (Mahabharata, Vana Parva, Ganguli sections 310 to 312).
- Behavior: the four were strong. They fell because they rushed. The one who stopped first saved everyone.
- Asana: Bakasana (baka = crane). People fall because they jump before they are set. Stop, set, look, breathe, then lift.

Point in one line a child can repeat: "Thirsty? Stop first. Then drink."

Toolkit pick: /hook (pattern interrupt) + Fork-First inside the /story beats, fail-hero-payoff. Why: the hook is a moment every viewer has lived (nose on the mat), so it stops the scroll with no news needed; the fork ("you are so thirsty, the voice says wait: do you drink?") lets the viewer choose before the fifth brother does. Fork-First was last used 2 Oct, Triple Hook was yesterday.
Other options: (1) Name-the-Feeling + /story: open on "You are not weak. You are thirsty." (2) Gervais close: Boonchu shows his own nose-to-mat fall, flat: "Twenty years. Still thirsty sometimes." (needs his OK on the self-joke).

## Hooks (/hook, answered myself)
Topic: rushing into an arm balance (Bakasana).
Consensus: "Arm balances need strong arms. Jump harder."
Where Boonchu sharpens it: most falls come from rushing the set-up, not from weak arms (Plato's draft: Boonchu must say if this matches 20 years of watching students).
1. Authority: "Twenty years of watching people fall from Bakasana. Same reason." (his claim: OK it first)
2. Contrarian: "You don't fall in Bakasana because you are weak."
3. Stakes: "Rush this asana and your nose pays for it."
4. Question: "Why do strong people fall on their face in Bakasana?"
5. Pattern interrupt: "You put your hands down. You jump. Your nose hits the floor."
Pick: 5. It opens with the trigger, every practitioner (10 years or 10 days) has felt it, it works with sound off as three short on-screen lines, and it sets the trap ("I need stronger arms") that the story snaps. Hook 2 goes on the cover: "Not weak. Thirsty." with "Thirsty" in gold.

## Script (60 s, spoken to camera, his spoken English)
HOOK (0-5 s)
"You put your hands down. You jump.
... Your nose hit the floor.
You think: I am weak.
No. You are thirsty."
[zoom punch: "nose" / "thirsty"] [card: a felt nose on a felt mat]

CONTEXT + MIRROR (5-12 s)
"You see the asana. You want it now.
Like when you are very, very thirsty... and you see water."
[card: ❌ "need stronger arms"]

ORDEAL: THE STORY, bedtime voice (12-40 s)
"At night I tell my son a story. From the Mahabharata.
Five brothers. Lost in the forest. So thirsty.
The youngest find a lake. Clear water. A crane stand there.
And a voice say: 'Wait. Answer my questions first. Then drink.'
[FORK, look at camera] You are so thirsty. Do you drink?
... He drink. He fall down. Not moving.
Second brother. Same voice. Same thirst. Drink. Fall.
Third brother. Fourth brother, the strongest one. Drink. Fall.
Then the oldest brother come. He is thirsty too.
But he stop. He listen. He say: 'Okay. Ask me.'
He answer every question.
And his four brothers... wake up. Like from a sweet sleep."
[silent beat, 2 s, soft water sound]
"Four strong brothers. They didn't fall because they were weak.
They fall because they rush."

TAKEAWAY: THE TURN + ASANA (40-55 s)
"In Ashtanga, we have this asana. Bakasana. Baka mean crane. Same bird.
[BOONCHU'S OWN CUE GOES HERE. Plato's draft only: 'Hands down. Knees high on the arms. Look forward, not down. One breath. Then lift.']
Most people fall... because they jump before they are ready.
The crane stand still first. Then it fly."

RETURN / PAYOFF (55-60 s)
"So next time your nose hit the floor...
Not weak. Just thirsty. Stop first. Then drink."

Payoff check: the hook opens "nose on the floor, am I weak?"; the last line closes it ("not weak, just thirsty, stop first"). One lesson only: rushing makes you fall, stopping first lets you go. The questions themselves are NOT taught (no Q&A list in the Reel), and the crane's true identity (Dharma, the brothers' father) is left out: one lesson.

Verify before filming:
- His own Bakasana cue (the one in the script is Plato's draft).
- Does "most people fall because they rush the set-up" match what he sees? If not, change the line in Takeaway.
- The fall shot: Boonchu himself, soft, onto a cushion, or a felt card. Never film a student falling (standing order).
- In the book the four brothers "dropped down dead" and rise again. The Reel says "fall down, not moving" and "wake up like from a sweet sleep" (the book's own picture: "as if they have sweetly awaked from slumber"). Keep it soft for the bedtime frame; never say "died" on screen.
- Ashtanga series: Bakasana sits in the Intermediate series (after Supta Vajrasana). Boonchu to confirm the name and place he wants on screen.
- Arjuna and Bhima are two of the four brothers (Arjuna was in the 1 and 7 Oct scripts, Bhima on 4 Oct). The Reel does not name them on purpose: "the strongest one" is enough.

## Sources (check before filming)
- Trigger: the viewer's own body (no news). No number, no study claim in the Reel.
- Story: Mahabharata, Vana Parva (Aranya Parva), Ganguli translation, sections 310 to 312:
  - Sec. 310: https://sacred-texts.com/hin/m03/m03310.htm (mirror: https://www.ibiblio.org/sripedia/ebooks/mb/m03/m03310.htm). Nakula sees "a crystal lake inhabited by cranes"; the voice: "O child, do not commit this rash act! This lake hath already been in my possession." He drinks and "dropped down dead"; then Sahadeva, Arjuna, Bhima the same.
  - Sec. 311: https://www.ibiblio.org/sripedia/ebooks/mb/m03/m03311.htm. The voice: "I am a crane, living on tiny fish." Yudhishthira: "I do not, O Yaksha, covet, what is already in thy possession!" Then the questions.
  - Sec. 312: https://www.ibiblio.org/sripedia/ebooks/mb/m03/m03312.htm. "the Pandavas rose up"; "as if they have sweetly awaked from slumber."
- Left out on purpose: the crane is really Dharma, Yudhishthira's father; the deer and the fire-sticks; Arjuna shooting arrows at the voice; the 30+ questions. One lesson only.
- Bakasana: baka = crane (Sanskrit). Ashtanga Intermediate series.

## 2026-10-10 · plato-dawn-five-times

# Plato · 10 Oct 2026 · daily Reel package
Hidden Stories of Ashtanga (no Ep. number until filmed; last posted Ep. 4)
"The One Who Comes Before the Sun"

Route: Lane A story with a Lane B door. Surya Namaskara is the first thing a beginner learns on Day 1, so the door is wide open. Last 3: frogs script (A, not filmed), Vasishtha script (A, not filmed), Arjuna script (A, not filmed) · next due: Lane B. EN/TH captions carry the promo. Chinese stays Lane A (no promo).
Habit: the core of The Habit Gap (same practice, same time) + #4 Sleep (early mornings; careful: no sleep promise anywhere).

Trigger note: no unused life note, no @Plato request. News checked: on Thu 8 Oct 2026 the WNBA named A'ja Wilson (Las Vegas Aces) its Most Valuable Player for the 2026 season. It is her 5th time, the most in league history, and her 3rd year in a row (first ever). 65 of 74 first-place votes. Plain words for the Reel: "best player of the year". No score, no team, no "MVP" in the spoken lines (PLAIN TRIGGER order). We do NOT know her morning routine, so the script never says what she does each day.

## The link (4 lines)
- Trigger: this week a basketball player won "best player of the year" for the fifth time. Nobody did that before.
- Story: Aruna's egg was broken too early, so he was born with only half a body. He still became the driver of the sun's chariot, "always seen in the hour of morning". He stands in front of the sun and takes its heat, so the world does not burn (Mahabharata, Adi Parva sections 16 and 24).
- Behavior: one time is a good year. Five times is a habit. The big thing people see (the sun, the trophy) stands on the quiet thing that comes first, every morning.
- Asana: Surya Namaskara A, five times, the first thing every Ashtanga morning. Same nine movements, same breath. It trains turning up before you feel ready.

Toolkit pick: Triple Hook (Proof + Whiplash) + /story beats, fail-hero-payoff. Why: the news is a clean Proof line a 10-year-old gets ("fifth time, nobody ever"), and the Whiplash ("one time is a good year, five times is a habit") moves it from her to the viewer's own mornings. Triple Hook was last used 7 Oct, not 3 days in a row.
Other options: (1) Vibe Samurai ladder: her five trophies / Aruna every dawn / you, five Surya Namaskaras tomorrow. (2) Reframe Unit: "Five trophies. That's about 1,800 mornings." (only if Boonchu wants a number; Plato did not use it because we do not know her mornings).

## Hooks (/hook, answered myself)
Topic: practice habit, coming back every morning.
Consensus: "Champions are born with talent."
Where Boonchu sharpens it: 20 years of Ashtanga, six days a week since 2008, and every one of those mornings started the same way: Surya Namaskara.
1. Authority: "Twenty years of mornings. The first five minutes never changed." (his claim: OK it first)
2. Contrarian: "Best player of the year, five times. That's not talent. That's mornings."
3. Stakes: "She was best player five times. Most of us can't do five mornings."
4. Question: "How do you become the best... five times?"
5. Pattern interrupt: "This week, a player won best of the year. Fifth time. Nobody ever did that."
Pick: 5, then the Whiplash line right after it. It opens with the trigger, makes sense with sound off and no basketball knowledge, and sets the trap ("she must be so talented") that the next line snaps ("one time is a good year; five times is a habit"). Hook 2 goes on the cover ("Not talent. Mornings." with "Mornings" in gold).

## Script (60 s, spoken to camera, his spoken English)
HOOK (0-5 s)
"This week, a basketball player win best player of the year. ... Fifth time.
Nobody ever do that before.
One time... is a good year. Five times... is a habit."
[card: five small gold trophies in a row, pop in one by one] [zoom punch: "Fifth time." / "habit"]

CONTEXT + MIRROR (5-13 s)
"Maybe you think: she is special. Born with it. Not me.
I start, I stop. I start again, I stop again."
[card: ❌ "born with it"]

ORDEAL: THE STORY, bedtime voice (13-38 s)
"At night I tell my son a story. From the Mahabharata.
A mother have two eggs. She wait, and wait... she cannot wait anymore.
She break one egg. Too early.
Inside, a baby bird. The top is ready... the legs, not finished.
His name is Aruna.
And this little one... every morning, he drive the sun across the sky.
The sun is so hot, it can burn the whole world.
So Aruna stand in front. He take the heat. So for us... the morning is soft.
Every morning. Not some mornings. Every morning."
[silent beat, 2 s, soft dawn sound]
"We all look at the sun. Nobody look at the one who come first."

TAKEAWAY: THE TURN + ASANA (38-55 s)
"In Ashtanga, every morning start the same. Surya Namaskara. Sun salutation.
Five times. Same nine movement. Same breath.
[BOONCHU'S OWN CUE GOES HERE: what he tells a student in Surya Namaskara A, 1-2 lines]
Not exciting. Nobody clap for it.
But the big asana later... stand on these five."

RETURN / PAYOFF (55-60 s)
"So I don't know her mornings. But I know this.
Five times is not luck. ... It's the morning."

Payoff check: the hook opens "fifth time, how?"; the last line closes it ("five times is not luck, it's the morning"). One lesson only: the big thing stands on the small thing you do first, every morning. The broken egg is only how Aruna was born; the Reel does NOT teach "be patient" (that was the 5 Oct hen Reel).

Verify before filming:
- His Surya Namaskara A cue in his own words (Plato did not write one).
- Hook 1 claim, if used.
- Never say what A'ja Wilson does each morning: we do not know. "I don't know her mornings" stays in.
- Aruna's body: say "not finished", never "broken", "crippled" or anything that sounds like a joke. He is the hero.
- Flag: eggs again (5 Oct hen Reel). Different lesson and asana, but if Boonchu posted the hen Reel this week, leave the egg out and start at "A bird called Aruna, born too early".

SFX at 40%, 0.4 s after the word: ping (Fifth time), chime (habit), click-soft (Too early), whoosh (Aruna), sparkle (Every morning), impact-bass-1 (It's the morning).   1.   2.   3.   4. 阿斯汤加的每一个早晨，也都从同一件事开始：拜日式A，五遍。同样的九个动作，同样的呼吸。
后面所有的体式，都站在这五遍上面。

## 2026-10-10 · plato-chat-upamanyu-rewrite

# Plato chat · 10 Oct 2026 · rewrite of the 8 Oct "Two Stars" script
Boonchu: "this is not 10 years old story telling style. i am 53 years old and i dont get the point of this."
Why it failed: no plot (two stars sit in the sky), three loose ideas, and the asana (alone on one hand) said the opposite of "together".
Process: 4 writers (keep Arundhati / new myth / animal fable / own-life trigger), 3 judges each (child test, 53-year-old viewer, facts and rules), 1 editor, 3 final checks, then 2 fresh readers on the edited script (child 7, viewer 7.5; their fixes applied below). 25 agents.
Scores: own-life + Upamanyu 21.5 (won) · tree spirits Jataka 74 19.5 · Manu's fish 17 FATAL (world flood while Thailand floods) · Arundhati jujubes 16 FATAL (Navasana repeat; husband away 12 years, another man keeps her company).

Point (one line): If you come every day, your teacher notices the day you don't.
Trigger: the viewer's own bad week (no news, no numbers).
Story: Upamanyu, Mahabharata, Adi Parva sec. 3 (Ganguli, Gutenberg 15474).
Asana: Samasthitih. Habit-system line: a teacher who notices when you're gone.

## Script (about 60 s, his spoken English)
HOOK (0-4 s), to camera
"One bad week. You don't come one morning. Then two.
Who notice?"

MIRROR (4-7 s)
"Nobody ask. So... you don't come back."

STORY, bedtime voice (7-36 s)
"A bedtime story from India.
A student. Upamanyu.
Every day, he look after the cows.
Every evening, he come back. Stand in front of his teacher... and bow." [small bow]
"One day... he fall into a well. A deep hole.
The sun go down.
The teacher look: 'Upamanyu... not back.'
He go to the forest. He shout: 'Upamanyu! Where are you?'
From the well: 'I'm here!'
The teacher help him. Upamanyu come home... and bow." [small bow]

(1 s silence)

TURN (37-43 s)
"How the teacher know?
Every evening, Upamanyu is there. One evening he is not... the teacher see it.
Your bad week... is the well."

ASANA (43-54 s), stand up and do it
"First thing in our yoga room: we stand. Feet together. Don't move.
Samasthitih. This is our bow." [small bow]
[BOONCHU'S OWN CUE: 5 words or less, about being here, not alignment]
"Every morning you stand there... your teacher see you."

PAYOFF (54-60 s)
"Alone... nobody notice.
With a teacher... the morning you don't come, your teacher notice."

About 155 spoken words. Loop: hook asks "Who notice?", payoff answers "your teacher".
On screen: AN OLD STORY FROM INDIA · UPAMANYU · SAMASTHITIH (Mahabharata goes in the caption).

## Sources (Ganguli, Adi Parva sec. 3, adi.txt lines 1786-1975)
- "Ayoda-Dhaumya had three disciples, Upamanyu, Aruni, and Veda."
- "'Go, my child, Upamanyu, look after the kine.'"
- "he returned in the evening to his preceptor's house and standing before him he saluted him respectfully." (Sanskrit 1.3.34: agratah sthitva namaschakre, "standing in front, he bowed")
- "he fell into a pit" ... "'Here I am at the bottom of a well.'"
- "when the sun was sinking down behind the summit of the western mountains, the preceptor observed to his disciples that Upamanyu was not yet come."
- "he went with his disciples into the forest and began to shout, saying, 'Ho Upamanyu, where art thou?'"
- "answered in a loud tone"
- "thus directed by his preceptor" ... "having gone to his preceptor's presence he saluted him and told him all."

## Flags
- The point changed: no couples, no study. If he still wants the couples Reel, option B = tree spirits (Jataka 74): spirits leave the forest for lone giant trees, a storm breaks every lone tree, the forest stands. Overlaps the 6 Oct doves Reel.
- In the book, the teacher is TESTING Upamanyu: he takes away all his food, the boy eats bitter leaves, goes blind, and that is why he falls. "The teacher help him" = he tells him to pray to the twin healer gods, who give his sight back; the book never says how he climbs out. The script leaves this out and adds nothing. Someone who knows the story could say the teacher caused the fall.
- Samasthitih is also the first and last frame of Surya Namaskara A (10 Oct Aruna Reel); hook overlaps the 6 Oct doves Reel. Post apart.
- Payoff promises only "your teacher notice" (his own Letter #1 line). No promise of calls or messages.
- Many China/Russia viewers practise at home or online: the caption must invite, not scold.
- Captions in 4 languages after his pick.

## v2 (Boonchu: "mention [the source] in there, and how these 2 characters relate to the main characters")
Checked by 15 agents (4 researchers, 2 skeptics each, writer, 2 readers), then 2 fresh readers on v2. Readers got lost when the family chain was SPOKEN inside the story (score 7, "I stopped listening to Upamanyu"), so: source spoken short + small card on screen, the link goes in the caption.

HOOK: "One bad week. You don't come to class one morning. Then two. Who notice?"
MIRROR: "Nobody ask. So... you don't come back."
STORY: "Bedtime story. From the Mahabharata. The first book." [ON SCREEN 2-3 s: MAHABHARATA · Book 1 (Adi Parva), section 3]
"A student, Upamanyu. Every day, he look after the cows. Every evening, he come back. Stand in front of his teacher... and bow." [small bow]
"One day... he fall into a hole. A well. The sun go down. The teacher look: 'Upamanyu... not back.' He go to the forest. He shout: 'Upamanyu! Where are you?' From the well: 'I'm here!' The teacher help him. Upamanyu come home... and bow." [small bow]
(1 s silence)
TURN: "How the teacher know? Every evening, Upamanyu is there. One evening, he is not. Your bad week... is the well."
ASANA: "Every morning, first thing: we stand. Feet together, hands together. Samasthitih. This is our bow." [small bow] "I see it every day." [BOONCHU'S OWN CUE]
PAYOFF: "Alone... nobody notice. Come every day... the day you don't, your teacher notice."
Optional spoken link (only at the very end, after the payoff): "P.S. Upamanyu's classmate later become the teacher of Arjuna's great-grandson."

## The link to the main characters (checked in Ganguli; line numbers in adi.txt, Gutenberg 15474)
- Upamanyu and his teacher never appear with Arjuna and his brothers. Upamanyu's name is only in Adi Parva sec. 3.
- Teacher Ayoda-Dhaumya: "had three disciples, Upamanyu, Aruni, and Veda" (1786-1787).
- Veda later became spiritual guide (Upadhyaya) of King Janamejaya (1994-1998); his student Utanka urged Janamejaya to hold the snake sacrifice against Takshaka, who killed his father Parikshit (2250-2280; sec. 4 calls it "one of the causes").
- Janamejaya = son of Parikshit (sec. 3), son of Abhimanyu (sec. 40, 49), son of Arjuna and Subhadra (sec. 95).
- At that sacrifice Vaisampayana, on Vyasa's order, "recited the whole of that history" (sec. 60). Do not say "first" told.
- NOT KNOWN, never say as fact: that Ayoda-Dhaumya is (or is not) Dhaumya the Pandavas' priest (sec. 185, "younger brother of Devala"); that this Upamanyu is the one Krishna visits (Anushasana 14, "descendant of Vyaghrapada"); that classmate Aruni, renamed Uddalaka, is Ashtavakra's grandfather (tradition only).
- In the book the teacher's help = "Glorify the twin Aswins"; never say he pulled him out.

## The Fish Who Stayed to Listen (matsyendra)

- Asana: Ardha Matsyendrasana, Intermediate series (after Bharadvajasana, before Eka Pada Sirsasana). Purna Matsyendrasana (Advanced A) is left out on purpose so the focus stays on one asana.
- Habit: #6 Calm the mind (breath-led practice: stay still, listen to the breath)
- Lane: Lane A (world: authority, depth). Route: Lane A · last posts: the stay-active carousels (open-door health theme; B5 'Never too late' scheduled 14:00 10 Oct) · next due: a Lane A Reel, so this is on route. Boonchu asked for this myth as the next Hidden Stories episode. Check the Postiz list before posting.
- Mirror: "Ten years on the mat. This bind still don't come. So I pull harder."
- Fail: You on the mat in Ardha Matsyendrasana. After years of practice the bind still does not come, so you pull, push, and move and move. The breath goes short [PENDING Boonchu cue, ask 1; 'the twist gets smaller' only if he confirms it]. You do more and hear less. The words stay 'you'. Footage: Boonchu acts it out (his OK, ask 5) or a puppet image. Never film a student failing.
- Hero: The fish near the shore. It does not swim away and does not move. It listens with full attention and learns yoga. Shiva sprinkles water on it, it becomes Matsyendra ("Lord of the Fishes"), and then he spreads yoga. The viewer is the fish: the one who listens with full attention is the one who later passes yoga on.
- Payoff: This closes the loop the hook opened ("Ten years. Still no bind... How?"): the fish had no arms and no legs, it did not move, it only listened with full attention, and it learned yoga. Spoken close: "You are not stuck. You are too busy pulling to listen." Then Boonchu's own cue [PENDING], one full breath in the twist on camera, "5 breaths" on screen, and cut on the exhale.
- Reveal: (silent beat) The fish... only listen. Full attention. That's how it learn.
- Plato's pick (workflow): Name-the-Feeling + Hero's Journey. The hook names the inner voice the judges said was missing ("ten years, still no bind, so I pull harder"), it stays in Lane A for 5 to 20 year practitioners, and the fish's road from listener to the one who teaches yoga points quietly at Mysore and the teacher program. It also moves away from today's Triple Hook + /story. It needs one true student story from Boonchu. Without one, run Five-Lever Hook (contrarian) + Hero's Journey.

### Bedtime story lines
- Long, long ago... Shiva, a great god, go to a quiet island. Nobody there.
- Parvati, his wife, is with him.
- He tell her the secrets of yoga.
- Near the shore, one fish... hear everything.
- It does not swim away. It does not move.
- It just listen. Full attention.
- Shiva look. He see... this fish, it already learn yoga.
- He put a little water on the fish.
- The fish become Matsyendra. 'Matsya'... means fish.
- Then Matsyendra go out... and teach yoga to many people.

### Options
**Name-the-Feeling + Hero's Journey**

Ten years. Still no bind. So you pull more... no?
Okay. A fish. No arms, no legs.
It learn yoga. How?

On screen: Years of practice. Still no bind. A fish knows why.

Body: The hook ends on 'How?'. Then 'Ardha Matsyendrasana. Ardha... means half.' Ordinary life and the call: story lines 1 to 4, stopping at 'hear everything'. Mirror: 'Like you. Same mat. Every morning.' Dark moment: cut to you pulling in the twist (footage is Boonchu acting it out or a puppet image, never a student). Mirror: 'You pull. Breath get short.' [PENDING Boonchu cue]. Then a draft joke by Plato that needs Boonchu's OK (skeleton 10): 'Good news: I still practise six days a week. Bad news: my spine only agree to twist on Tuesday.' Mirror: 'You do more... and hear less.' Silent beat. The test, held until now: the reveal_line (it stands in for story lines 5 and 6). Return with a gift: story lines 7 to 10, then the mirror 'The one who listen... later, the one who teach.' Then one true 2-sentence student story (required, ask 4), the close 'You are not stuck. You are too busy pulling to listen.', Boonchu's cue [PENDING], and one full breath on camera with '5 breaths' on screen, cut on the exhale. If there is no true student story, the pick becomes Five-Lever Hook (contrarian) + Hero's Journey, with the hook 'You don't get this bind by pulling more.' [PENDING Boonchu cue].

It follows this week's stay-active carousels (B5 'Never too late' scheduled 14:00 10 Oct; check it published in Postiz). The viewer walks the fish's road from quiet listener to the one who teaches yoga, which points quietly to Mysore and the teacher path without a pitch.

**Vibe Samurai (flat statement, ladder of 3) + Gervais deadpan close**

A fish learn yoga. It did not move. It only listen.
B.K.S. Iyengar tell this old story in his book, Light on Yoga.

On screen: A fish learned yoga. It just listened.

Body: Flat statement, then credit the source: Iyengar tells this old story (credit him for the story only, never for how the asana is done). Then a ladder of 3: the beginner who grabs the foot, the 10-year practitioner who forces the bind, and you, pulling harder every year. Silent beat, then the turn 'It isn't doing more. It's listening more.', the screenshot line 'The fish only listened. That was the lesson.', and a deadpan close on Boonchu talking too much (skeleton 21 line, a draft by Plato that needs his OK). Boonchu's cue [PENDING] still comes last. The pitch goes only in the caption.

Why: This is the judges' strongest sound-off line. The ladder moves toward the viewer and ends on their own voice (pulling harder every year), and crediting Iyengar early labels it honestly as an old story he tells.

### Five hooks
- authority: Twenty years of Ashtanga. Six days a week since 2008. And the hero of this story... a fish.
- contrarian: You don't get this bind by pulling more. [PENDING Boonchu cue: say it only if he agrees]
- stakes: You can fight this twist for ten more years. Or stop... and listen, like the fish.
- question: How does a fish learn yoga?
- pattern interrupt (joke skeleton 21, The confession; the checked joke angle on Boonchu's talking): I'll be honest. Twenty years of practice... and I still cannot stay quiet for five breaths in this twist. (cut / card) Ardha Matsyendrasana. 'Ardha'... half. 'Matsya'... fish.

### Facts used
- The fish story is a legend that B.K.S. Iyengar retells ('It is related that...') under Ardha Matsyendrasana I. It is not in the Hatha Yoga Pradipika. (B.K.S. Iyengar, Light on Yoga (George Allen & Unwin, 1966); wording checked in The Illustrated Light on Yoga, entry 55, p. 112 (archive.org copy), via ep_candidates.json key 'matsyendra'; same passage in the full-text copy at scratchpad fc3/loy.txt (Ardha Matsyendrasana I entry))
- Shiva went to a lonely island (spoken: 'a quiet island. Nobody there.') and explained the mysteries (spoken: 'the secrets') of yoga to Parvati, his consort (spoken: 'his wife'). (Iyengar, The Illustrated Light on Yoga p. 112: 'once Lord Siva went to a lonely island and explained to his consort Parvati the mysteries of Yoga')
- A fish near the shore heard everything with concentration and stayed still (motionless) while listening. (Iyengar, The Illustrated Light on Yoga p. 112: 'A fish near the shore heard everything with concentration and remained motionless while listening')
- Shiva saw that the fish had learned yoga and sprinkled water on it (spoken: 'put a little water on the fish'). (Iyengar, The Illustrated Light on Yoga p. 112: 'Siva, realizing that the fish had learnt Yoga, sprinkled water upon it')
- The fish became Matsyendra, 'Lord of the Fishes' (card only), and then spread the knowledge of yoga (spoken: 'teach yoga to many people'). (Iyengar, The Illustrated Light on Yoga p. 112: 'the fish gained divine form and became Matsyendra (Lord of the Fishes) and thereafter spread the knowledge of Yoga')
- 'Ardha' means half; 'Matsya' means fish. (Iyengar, Light on Yoga, full-text copy (scratchpad fc3/loy.txt): Ardha Matsyendrasana I entry, 'Ardha means half'; Matsyasana entry, 'Matsya means a fish')
- Ardha Matsyendrasana is in the Ashtanga Intermediate series, after Bharadvajasana and before Eka Pada Sirsasana. Purna Matsyendrasana is in Advanced A. (AshtangaYoga.info Intermediate sheet and the Robson chart, plus the AshtangaYoga.info third-series list, as checked in ep_candidates.json key 'matsyendra')
- Boonchu started Ashtanga 20 years ago and has practised strictly six days a week since 2008. (plato.md standing order 2026-10-04 'BOONCHU'S NUMBERS' (confirmed by him); task context)
- Boonchu tells these myths to his 5-year-old son as bedtime stories. (plato.md standing order 2026-09-28 and Life notes 2026-09-28; task context)
- Ep. 4 (Tittibhasana) was filmed and edited on the night of 2 Oct and marked POSTED in the 3 Oct note. The exact post date is not recorded. (plato.md standing order 2026-10-03 and Stories used 2026-10-02)
- The Flexible-or-Stiff Reel's self-joke close drew its first comment within minutes. (plato.md 'What worked' 2026-10-04 ('Quite an anti climax at the end' plus 4 laughing emojis))
- Stay-active carousel B5 'Never too late' is scheduled for 14:00 10 Oct and is not yet marked PUBLISHED. (plato.md Stories used 2026-10-09 stay-active x5 (cmv0xfm1h01tvnp0ywwq0g6mp))

### Risks and fixes
- Less 'hidden': serious practitioners may know Iyengar's retelling. Lean on the name meaning (Matsya = fish, 'Lord of the Fishes' on the card) and the listening lesson. Label it plainly as a legend on a card ('A legend, as told by B.K.S. Iyengar in Light on Yoga') and in the caption. Credit Iyengar only for the story, never for how the asana is done (his Ardha Matsyendrasana I sits on the left foot, which is not the Ashtanga form).
- Leave out: the version where the parents throw the baby into the sea, Feuerstein's 'sleepy spouse' line, and the Hatha Yoga Pradipika lines about destroying 'the most deadly diseases' and awakening Kundalini. Never say stillness or this twist keeps you young or heals anything.
- Shiva and Parvati are deities, and Matsyendra is honoured as a siddha (worshipped in Nepal as Machhindranath). No joke goes near any of them or near the fish. The joke stays on Boonchu only. Pictures show no divine faces: Shiva as a carved wooden hand putting water on the fish, Parvati as a felt shawl seen from behind, and Matsyendra after the change as a glowing felt fish outline. Keep every Isle of Dogs handmade cue (stop-motion puppet film still in the style of Wes Anderson's Isle of Dogs, a handmade tabletop diorama, felt and wool puppets with glassy bead eyes, cut-felt waves, flat front-on camera, visible fibers).
- 'No arms, no legs' is our framing, not part of the legend. Never add details the source does not give: how long the fish listened, what it felt, what Shiva said word for word, 'all', 'little', 'silent', 'never moved'.
- The fix is a gap: there is no Boonchu cue for Ardha Matsyendrasana on file. Every [PENDING Boonchu cue] line (the fail's 'breath goes short', the contrarian hook, option B's stuck middle, caption line 3) stays unfilmed and unposted until he gives his own words. Do not post the caption with a placeholder.
- Intermediate reaches fewer viewers than Primary. Open on the feeling (years in, still no bind), not the asana name, so Primary practitioners stay.
- Lane: 'too old to learn' is a Lane B fear-buster ('I'm too old'). It is kept out of this Lane A Reel and saved for a Lane B Reel. Never state Boonchu's age.
- Episode number: Boonchu asked for this as the next ep. Fill in Ep. 5 on post day only if nothing has posted since Ep. 4 and this is filmed before the Foolish Old Man / Supta Kurmasana script (that script then takes the next number).
- The unfilmed Bharadvajasana script is also an Intermediate twist. If both get filmed, space them apart.
- The mirror can turn intellectual. Keep the turn in the body (the pulling, the breath going short) so viewers feel 'that's me', not just 'good point'.
- Keep the student-to-teacher hint quiet: no 180K program or price in the spoken video. The caption carries only 'Come learn Ashtanga / Aybkk.net'.
