## The Bear Who Said Ninety (jambavan)

- Asana: Hanumanasana, Advanced A (third series). The asana recalls the leap across the sea that comes soon after this scene (Sundara Kanda 1).
- Habit: #9 Do it with others ('a quiet room of people who show up'; somebody next to you sees what you can't), landing on the core idea 'a teacher who notices'. No second habit: #2 was dropped because next to 'keep your ninety' it read as a promise that practice holds off ageing.
- Lane: Route: Lane A · last 3 posts: stay-active carousels 3, 4, 5 (10 Oct; Lane B, with the new-student promo) · next due: Lane A is on route (the last 3 posts are all Lane B). By the plan dates, the Lane B Day 1 to Day 30 series starts 13 Oct. Note: this is the 5th Lane A script in a row (Arjuna, Vasishtha, frogs, dawn, this one), and the 9 and 10 Oct route lines said Lane B next. Lane B is due after 2 Lane A posts.
- Mirror: "I used to do more. Now I do a little less. All I see is what I lost... maybe my best practice is behind me."
- Fail: Everyone counts only their own jump. The eight leaders call out 10, 20 and up to 80. The oldest one can do 90, but 'by that much endeavour that task will be unaccomplished' (4-65-17). The quiet one sits alone and says nothing (4-66-2). The viewer's version: counting only the ten you lose.
- Hero: Jambavan the bear, 'the oldest one among all of them' (4-65-10). First he appreciates the others (4-65-10). Then he says his ninety out loud, with no shame (4-65-13). Then he looks up from his own number to the quiet one and says 'Arise... leap over this vast ocean' (4-66-36). He is the hidden hero behind Hanuman's leap.
- Payoff: Hanuman gets up and jumps the whole sea. Hanumanasana is named for Hanuman and remembers his leap. For the viewer: your ninety still counts (Jambavan's ninety is more than all eight before him), and while you only see the ten you lose, somebody next to you sees the ninety. That needs someone who sees you every morning: that is Mysore, a teacher plus a quiet room of people who show up (#9). It also points to the 180K program (learning to see a student) without a pitch. The practice landing is Boonchu's own Hanumanasana cue, then the last line flips the hook: 'You see the ten you lose. Somebody next to you see the ninety.'
- Reveal: (after the silent beat, Hanuman's jump and the turn) Keep your ninety.
- Plato's pick (workflow): Win/Loss Frame + Vibe Samurai (number ladder). Why: the 10-to-90 card is the strongest sound-off hold, and the hook is the viewer's own inner voice. Win/Loss Frame has never been a pick (it was only offered as an option on 4 Oct). Vibe Samurai's ladder was last used 8 Oct (Two Stars), so it repeats within 3 days, but not 3 days in a row. /story, which was in every pick from 7 to 10 Oct, gets a rest, and the pick also avoids today's Triple Hook.

### Bedtime story lines
- Long time ago... many monkeys stand at the edge of the sea. They have to go to the other side.
- One hundred yojana. Yojana... old Indian way to say how far. One hundred... very, very far.
- One monkey say: I can jump ten. Next one: twenty. Thirty... forty... all the way to eighty.
- (to camera) Everybody count only their own jump. Same on the mat.
- Then Jambavan speak. He is a bear. The oldest one of all.
- He say: when I was young, nobody strong like me. Now... I can do ninety.
- Ninety. More than everybody before him. But not one hundred... (to camera) Not enough. You know this feeling.
- Then he look at Hanuman, the monkey god. Sitting alone. Quiet. 'Hanuman... why you say nothing?'
- (to camera) Sometimes the quiet one in the room... is you.
- 'I am old now. But you... you have everything. Get up. Jump over this sea.'

### Options
**Win/Loss Frame + Vibe Samurai (number ladder)**

Your practice used to be a hundred. Now... ninety.
And all you see is the ten you lose.
In an old Indian story, the Ramayana, the oldest one say his number out loud. Ninety.

On screen: Your practice used to be 100. Now 90.

Body: A flat line, then the source as small on-screen text only ('Valmiki Ramayana, Book 4', never spoken). Then the bedtime lines, one mirror line per beat. The numbers beat (story lines 3 to 7) is spoken as 3 rungs while the card counts 10 to 80, then 90 in gold, then a silent '?': 'The younger ones: ten... twenty... up to eighty.' / 'The oldest one, Jambavan the bear: ninety. More than all eight before him.' / 'And you, on your mat: you count only the ten you lose.' The third rung is that beat's mirror line. Lines 8 to 10 end on 'Get up. Jump over this sea.' (silent beat) 'And Hanuman get up... and jump the whole sea.' Turn: 'It's not the ten he's missing. It's Hanuman he see.' Reveal: 'Keep your ninety.' Then [your own Hanumanasana cue, waiting for you]. Close, the flip of the hook: 'You see the ten you lose. Somebody next to you see the ninety.' No promo and no price, spoken or in the caption.

Why: The numbers are the story, so the 10-to-90 card works like a progress bar: people stay to see it finish, even with sound off. 'Used to be 100, now 90' is the viewer's own inner voice in one line, and it stays the viewer's (Jambavan only says his ninety). It is on the theme of this week's stay-active carousels (fit when old, never too late).

**Fork-First Story + Hero's Journey (5 quick steps)**

You have the most years on the mat. Everybody look at you.
The sea is one hundred. You can do ninety.
What you say?

On screen: Sea: 100. You: 90. What do you say?

Body: A 1 s pause, then the answer as a quick Hero's Journey: the monkeys at the sea (ordinary life), one hundred yojana (the call), 10 to 80 (the test), his ninety is not enough and Hanuman sits quiet (the dark moment), then he appreciates them all, says his ninety with no shame, and tells the quiet one 'Get up. Jump over this sea.' (the gift). One mirror line per beat. Then the silent beat, Hanuman's jump, the turn, 'Keep your ninety.', your Hanumanasana cue and the closing flip.

Why: It puts a 10 to 20-year practitioner in the shoes of the one with the most years on the mat at the moment of choice, without calling anyone old. His answer (say your number, then see the other one) is a Mysore teacher's job, so it points to the 180K program with no pitch. Hero's Journey (last used 4 Oct) replaces /story, which was in every pick from 7 to 10 Oct. Flag: Fork-First was also the hook tool of 'Go Small' (2 Oct), on the same asana.

**Joke skeleton 10 (Good news, bad news) + Honest Flaw, body Hero's Journey (the oldest one as the hidden mentor)**

Good news: twenty years, still on the mat six days a week.
Bad news: small letters, low light... I need glasses. (glasses on)
Everybody lose something. In an old Indian story, the Ramayana, the oldest one say it out loud.

On screen: Good news: 6 days a week. Bad news: glasses.

Body: The joke, one true line, then the Hero's Journey in 5 quick steps: the sea, the call, the ladder 10 to 90, Hanuman's silence, and the oldest one's 'Get up. Jump.' as the mentor's gift. One mirror line per beat. Then the silent beat, Hanuman's jump, the turn, 'Keep your ninety.', your Hanumanasana cue and the closing flip.

Why: Boonchu owns the loss first, with a true fact and a laugh, so 'losing something' lands on him and never on the viewer. The self-joke close on the 4 Oct Flexible-or-Stiff Reel drew the first comment within minutes (plato.md What worked; that was a joke at the end, not in the hook). The glasses are also a callback to Ep. 1 for followers.

### Five hooks
- Authority: An old Indian story, the Ramayana. Before the big jump... the oldest one say his number.
- Contrarian: People say: when you get older, stop counting. In an old Indian story, the Ramayana, the oldest one count out loud. Ninety. More than all eight before him.
- Stakes: Count only the ten you lose... and one morning you stop coming to the mat.
- Question: Ten. Twenty. Thirty... eighty. Ninety. So who jump the sea?
- Pattern interrupt (joke skeleton 10: Good news, bad news): Good news: twenty years, still on the mat six days a week. Bad news: small letters... glasses. (glasses on) Everybody lose something. In an old Indian story, the Ramayana, the oldest one say it out loud.

### Ask Boonchu
- Your own Hanumanasana cue, in your words: what you tell a student, and what you look for that they cannot see themselves. The practice landing and caption line 3 wait for this, and Plato will not write one for you.
- Your words on what you watch for when you walk a Mysore room, so the closing line 'Somebody next to you see the ninety' is your voice, not Plato's.
- Your real 'ninety': is there something your body does a little less now than before? Use it only if you want to say it. If not, we use only the true reading-glasses line. We will never use the '10 cm lost from Hanumanasana' line in the research note, because it is invented.
- Your age on camera: 52, 53 or 54? (Telegram says 52, the 28 Sep note says 53, the joke page says 54.) Until you answer, the joke says 'twenty years, still on the mat six days a week' and gives no age.
- Is it OK to use the reading glasses again (they were in Ep. 1) as the skeleton 10 joke and a series callback?
- Has anything posted since Ep. 4 (3 Oct)? The Foolish Old Man / Supta Kurmasana script was given Ep. 5 on 3 Oct, and plato.md records no filming or posting of it. Whichever one you film and post first takes Ep. 5. The caption says 'Ep. N' until then.
- This would be a third Hanuman script in 10 days, and a second Hanumanasana script after 'Go Small' (Surasa). If you film this one, should 'Go Small' be dropped or moved to another asana?

- Promo: this caption follows the Lane A format, so it has no promo. The 9 and 10 Oct scripts carried the promo in EN/TH. If you want it here too, add this before 'Aybkk.net', EN/TH only, never Chinese: 'New students: buy 1, get 1 free on 1, 2 or 3 months, till 11 Nov. Comment aybkk 👇'

Your practice used to be 100. Now 90.
Hidden Stories of Ashtanga, Ep. N

### Facts used
- The eight leaders say how far they can jump: 10, 20, 30, 40, 50, 60, 70 and 80 yojanas (Gaja, Gavaksha, Sharabha, Rishabha, Gandhamadana, Mainda, Dvivida, Sushena) (Valmiki Ramayana, Kishkindha Kanda, Sarga 65 (4-65-3 onward), trans. Desiraju Hanumanta Rao, valmikiramayan.net (2003, revised 2005))
- The sea is a hundred yojanas wide; a yojana is an old unit of distance (Valmiki Ramayana 4-65-19 (the number is said by Angada, who is left out); checked candidate note)
- Jambavan is 'the oldest one among all of them', and first appreciates the others for their numbers; he is a bear (4-65-10 (oldest; 'appreciating them'); bear: translator's note at 4-65-18 ('Though Jambavanta is a bear') and 4-66-38, valmikiramayan.net (both pages re-read 10 Oct 2026))
- 'My energy in youth was unmatchable and unsurpassable' (his own words about himself; 'old and decrepit' is never spoken about the viewer) (Valmiki Ramayana 4-65-16)
- He gives no number for his youth: his capability 'was not definitely limited only thereunto'. Used only to block the invented '100 then 90' line for Jambavan; '100 to 90' is only ever the viewer's (Valmiki Ramayana 4-65-14 (re-read 10 Oct 2026))
- 'We can go up to ninety yojana-s, undoubtedly' (Valmiki Ramayana 4-65-13)
- Ninety is not enough: 'by that much endeavour that task will be unaccomplished' (Valmiki Ramayana 4-65-17)
- Hanuman sits quiet and alone: 'why do not you mumble something, why do you resort to a calmly loneliness' (Valmiki Ramayana, Kishkindha Kanda 4-66-2)
- 'I am now aged and my valiance is completely lessened ... you alone are endowed with all endowments among us' (Valmiki Ramayana 4-66-34)
- 'Arise, oh, lion-like monkey, leap over this vast ocean' (Valmiki Ramayana 4-66-36)
- Angada says he can vault the hundred yojanas but is not sure he can come back. This is used only to block a wrong line, never in the script (Valmiki Ramayana 4-65-19, Sarga 65 page re-read 10 Oct 2026)
- Hanumanasana is in Advanced A (third series); it is dedicated to Hanuman and remembers his leaps (Placement: AshtangaYoga.info 3rd series list + Boonchu 30 Sep (plato.md). Leap link: B.K.S. Iyengar, Light on Yoga (1966), no. 169: 'dedicated to Hanuman and commemorates his fabulous leaps'.)
- The leap itself comes soon after this scene, in Sundara Kanda 1 (Kishkindha Kanda Sarga 67 comes between) (Valmiki Ramayana, Sundara Kanda 1 (also cited for the Surasa scene in plato.md, 2 Oct 'Go Small'); check report 10 Oct)
- Boonchu: 20 years of Ashtanga, six days a week since 2008, 14 years teaching in Bangkok, needs reading glasses for close reading in low light. His age is not said on camera until he confirms it (plato.md 4 Oct standing orders (confirmed by him) and 28 Sep life note; age clash in plato.md Stories used, 10 Oct (Telegram 52, notes 53, joke page 54))
- 'We cannot see ourselves' lands with experienced viewers (plato.md 'What worked': practitioner comment on the 4 Oct adjustment Reel)
- The self-joke close on the 4 Oct Flexible-or-Stiff Reel drew the first comment within minutes (plato.md 'What worked', 4 Oct)
- This week's five stay-active carousels were on the theme fit when old, never too late (no likes or comments are recorded, so 'most-loved' is not claimed) (plato.md Stories used, 9 Oct stay-active x5)
- Tool rotation: Vibe Samurai ladder last picked 8 Oct; /story in every pick 7 to 10 Oct; Fork-First picked 2 Oct ('Go Small', Hanumanasana); Hero's Journey picked 4 Oct; Win/Loss Frame only offered as an option on 4 Oct, never picked (plato.md Stories used + 'Toolkit pick' lines in bots/posts/2026-10-02 to 2026-10-10 (checked 10 Oct))

### Risks and fixes
- FACT TRAP: in 4-65-19 Angada says he CAN vault the hundred yojanas (he is just not sure he can come back). So we must never say Jambavan's 90 is 'the farthest jump in the room' or that 'nobody could do 100'. The only true line is 'more than all eight before him'.
- 'The old one steps aside for the young' would confirm 'too old'. The reveal talks to the viewer ('Keep your ninety'), and Jambavan is the one who makes the leap happen, not one who quits. 'Old and decrepit' stays out of the spoken lines. 'I am old now' is only his own line about himself, and no student is ever called old. Option 2 now says 'the most years on the mat', never 'you are the oldest one'.
- Freshness: this is the third Hanuman script in 10 days (after 'Go Small' with Surasa and Bhima's tail; plato.md records no filming or posting of either). It is the second Hanumanasana script, and the leap is the same one where Surasa appears. The sea also comes back after Ep. 4. To soften this, the hero is Jambavan, Hanuman stays silent until the last line, and the hook avoids the 'stuck split' line from 'Go Small'.
- Hanumanasana is in Advanced A, so many viewers aged 40 to 60 may feel 'not for me'. The practice point is 'seeing what you can't see yourself', to be filled from Boonchu's answer, never 'advanced only', and the brand stays welcoming.
- Purists say 'the teachers never needed myths', and Hanumanasana already carries a known story (Iyengar ties it to Hanuman's leaps). The landing must be Boonchu's own Hanumanasana cue, so the full script waits for it. Caption line 3 is a placeholder for his words.
- Content safety: leave out Angada (a prince), Sugriva (a king), why they cross the sea (Sita and Ravana), the translator's 'fear of Ravana' note, Emperor Bali (4-65-15), and Hanuman's birth story and curse (Sarga 66). 'King of bears' (4-66-38) is a citation only, never spoken. The bedtime 'why' stays only 'they have to go to the other side'.
- Never use the research note's joke about '10 cm lost from Boonchu's Hanumanasana' (it is invented). Skeleton 10 uses only the true reading-glasses fact, needs his OK because the glasses were already in Ep. 1, and says no age until he confirms it.
- No medical claims: never say Hanumanasana keeps you young or is safe at any age. 'Keep your ninety' is about practice, not a health promise. Habit #2 was dropped for this reason.
- Hanuman is a deity, so show him with respect and never as a comic image. Image notes: Hanuman shown from behind, a carved wooden figure sitting on a cut-felt rock, no puppet face; Jambavan as a felt bear puppet; every handmade Isle of Dogs cue kept.
- Ep. number: the caption says 'Ep. N'. N = last posted + 1, set when filmed: Ep. 5 if Foolish Old Man is still unposted and this one posts first.
- CONFLICT (promo): checker 1 asked to add the promo line before 'Aybkk.net'; checker 2 asked for no promo in a Lane A caption. Kept the Lane A caption format from the brief (no promo), removed 'The promo stays in the caption' from Option 1, and put the exact promo line in ask_boonchu for Boonchu to add to EN/TH only if he wants it (never Chinese).
- MERGED (route): checker 1 said Lane A is on route; checker 2 said next due is Lane B (Day 1 to Day 30 from 13 Oct). The route line now says both: Lane A is on route now by the mix rule, and the Lane B Day 1 to Day 30 series starts 13 Oct by the plan dates.
- FIXED: Jambavan no longer says '100 then 90' or 'lost ten'. The hook and on-screen text say 'Your practice used to be 100. Now 90.' (checker 2), so the 100 is clearly the viewer's. Checker 1's on-screen 'Used to do more. Now 90.' was not used: with the sound off, 'Now 90' reads as an age. Jambavan only says his number, ninety (4-65-13).
- FIXED: turn line merged from both checkers: 'It's not the ten he's missing. It's Hanuman he see.' (the ten is how far short of the sea he is, not ten lost to age).
- FIXED: 'Ramayana' is explained as 'an old Indian story' in every spoken hook; 'Valmiki Ramayana, Book 4' is on screen only. The Authority hook no longer says he 'stand up' (not in Sarga 65).
- FIXED: Contrarian hook sets no age against the myth (no 'after forty', no 'go down'). The Stakes hook no longer uses the sea metaphor at second 0.
- FIXED: 'fifty-two' became 'twenty years, still on the mat six days a week'. 'Everybody lose a little' became 'Everybody lose something' (4-66-34 says 'completely lessened').
- FIXED: one mirror line after each story beat (MIRROR RULE 2). Bedtime line 3 became 'old Indian way to say how far' and line 6 became 'nobody strong like me'. To stay at 10 lines, 'why you say nothing?' joins the 'look at Hanuman' line, and the second mirror sits on the 'not one hundred' line.
- FIXED: reveal order. Story, then 'Get up. Jump over this sea.', silent beat, Hanuman's jump, the turn, 'Keep your ninety.', Boonchu's cue, then the closing flip. 'Let somebody see your hundred' is gone (it made the viewer Hanuman).
- FIXED: Vibe Samurai ladder is 3 spoken rungs ending on 'you'. Checker 2's 'The young ones' is written 'The younger ones', because the text only says Jambavan is the oldest. Rung 2 also names him and keeps the one true comparison.
- FIXED: Option 2 is no longer Fork-First + /story (the 'Go Small' combo, and /story ran 7 to 10 Oct). The body is now Hero's Journey, and the shared Fork-First hook tool is flagged.
- FIXED: the leap link is now cited to Iyengar, Light on Yoga no. 169. 'Hanumanasana IS that leap', 'the most-told leap in yoga' and 'right after this scene' are removed everywhere, including the risks.
- FIXED: caption says 'Ep. N'. Line 3 is one line with a placeholder for Boonchu's point. The Plato-made 'back leg' cue is removed from the caption and the risks.
- FIXED: plato_pick reason corrected (Vibe Samurai was used 8 Oct). 'Most-loved theme' and 'fastest comments' are now worded to match plato.md.
- FIXED: bear source corrected (translator's note at 4-65-18 and 4-66-38, not 4-65-10), and 'praises' became 'appreciates' to match 4-65-10. The #9 gloss now follows direction.md, and #2 is deleted. Route line added. 'Never filmed' became 'no filming or posting recorded in plato.md'.

## 2026-10-10 · plato

Trigger: 8 Oct, a basketball player won "best player of the year" for the 5th time. Nobody did that before (CBS Sports, ESPN).
Story: Aruna (Mahabharata, Adi Parva 16 and 24). Born too early with half a body, he still drives the sun, "always seen in the hour of morning". Asana: Surya Namaskara A, 5 times every morning.
Hook: "This week, a player won best of the year. Fifth time. Nobody ever did that." Then: "One time is a good year. Five times is a habit." Lane A story, Lane B door, promo in EN/TH caption.
Full package: posts/2026-10-10-plato-dawn-five-times.md. Waiting on Boonchu's own Surya Namaskara A cue.

## 2026-10-10 · carnegie-research-men-and-yoga

# Why fewer men practise yoga (and Ashtanga)

FACT = seen in a source. GUESS = my inference.

## 1. The answer

Your "not educated" guess is the weak one: no study shows less schooling keeps men away. What does: the idea that yoga is for women, a "men go to the gym" mindset, and fear of looking stiff next to flexible women (small studies only). The biggest reason you missed: many men are just not interested and pick the gym or a sport.

## 2. Your 4 guesses

**"For girls": TRUE** (strong in the West, little data for Asia).
- FACT: 21 Australian men who don't do yoga named two barriers: they prefer other exercise, and yoga is seen as feminine, plus "bloke" culture ([Cagas 2020](https://www.qigonginstitute.org/abstract-print/16156)).
- FACT: "Men go to the gym; girls go to the yoga class. That's how things go." (James, 28, now practises; [Cagas 2024](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11580054/), 11 men, 4 women.)

**"Afraid of stretching": HALF TRUE.** They fear looking stiff, not the stretch.
- FACT: UK adults not interested in yoga most often said "not flexible enough", women too ([YouGov 2015](https://yougov.com/en-gb/articles/11434-yoga-reduces-stress)). So it cannot explain the gap alone.
- FACT: Men compared themselves "to the more flexible women" and felt incompetent (Cagas 2024). A Russian teacher hears: "When I get more flexible, then I'll come" ([Lifehacker 2020](https://lifehacker.ru/zanyatiya-jogoj-dlya-muzhchin/)).

**"Don't know / not educated": "don't know" TRUE, "not educated" NOT SUPPORTED.**
- FACT: "Most male participants did not realize the existence of many different yoga styles" (Cagas 2024).
- FACT: US college graduates do more yoga, but that is everyone, not split by sex ([Birdee 2008](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC2533368/), n=31,044). GUESS: men have wrong ideas, not less school.

**"Mentality": TRUE.**
- FACT: 155 Canadian men, mostly students: the more they held to traditional male norms, the less they planned to try yoga ([Motzkus thesis 2022](https://uwindsor.scholaris.ca/server/api/core/bitstreams/4f7eb6d7-af7f-4fa9-9df8-78bcf2d2c731/content)).
- FACT: "Like you wanna go in there, be good at it straight away" (Oliver, 36, Cagas 2024).

## 3. Reasons you did not list (strongest first)

1. **No interest.** FACT: 351 US students. Non-practising men: no interest 47%, prefer other exercise 22%, no time 20%. Women's top reason: no time, 47% ([Garnsey 2025](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jbmt.2025.01.055); average age 19). GUESS: "no interest" is often the polite form of "it's for girls".
2. **Nobody brought them.** FACT: men started because a partner, friend, family member or physio pushed them. The paper is titled "Men Need Role Models" (Cagas 2024).
3. **The only man in the room.** FACT: "I didn't try yoga earlier as I didn't feel comfortable going to yoga as the only man in the studio." (Liam, 30, Cagas 2024). In China, a gym man in a yoga class "需要很大的勇气" (needs a lot of courage) ([36Kr 2021](https://www.36kr.com/p/1398510405483529)).

## 4. Country differences

| Where | What we know | Strength |
|---|---|---|
| West | US 2022: 23.3% of women vs 10.3% of men ([CDC 2024](https://www.cdc.gov/nchs/products/databriefs/db501.htm)). No marked change in 20 years ([Davies 2024](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11217305/), n=134,959). | Strong |
| China | Women 94.9% of users ([iResearch 2018](https://www.jiemian.com/article/2438265.html)). Men 13.6% of fans, 12.2% of teachers; they like strength-led classes like Rocket yoga (36Kr 2021). | Weak |
| Russia | One St Petersburg survey: 70% women, 30% men ([Snob blog 2025](https://snob.ru/profile/414702/blog/3103824/), 1,000+). A teacher blames "stereotypes that it's a girls' activity" (Lifehacker 2020). | Very weak |
| Thailand | No data found. Editors say men "might feel a bit shy and not know how to start" ([Sanook Men 2024](https://www.sanook.com/men/88937/)). | None |
| India | Men practise as much or more: 4.7% vs 2.3% ([NNMS 2020](https://www.ncdirindia.org/nnms/resources/Chapter_4_6.pdf), about 12,000 households); 11.9% vs 11.7% ([Mishra 2020](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7168227/), n=112,735). GUESS: "for girls" is learned culture, not yoga. | Good |

Ashtanga only: FACT: 17.5% men (29 of 166, a self-selected online survey; [Ramirez-Duran 2022](https://internationaljournalofwellbeing.org/index.php/ijow/article/download/2027/1065/8541)). Too small to compare with national numbers, but there is no sign Ashtanga draws more men.

Ashtanga history: FACT: in 1930s Mysore, young men scorned yoga and preferred the "more fashionable and 'manly'" bodybuilding gym of K.V. Iyer, next to Krishnamacharya's yogashala ([Wellcome Collection 2018](https://wellcomecollection.org/stories/yoga-adapts-to-time-and-place), citing Singleton, *Yoga Body*, 2010). The palace yogashala trained boys (weak source: [Bhavan's Journal 2024](https://esamskriti.com/e/History/Indian-History/How-MAHARAJA-of-Mysore-support-to-T-Krishnamacharya-led-to-the-REVIVAL-of-Modern-Day-YOGA---1.aspx)). So the doubt was never "it's for girls" back then. It was "it's not as manly as the gym". Same fight, 90 years old.

## 5. What this means for money

**Lane B (Bangkok open door): worth a small test.**
- FACT: men are about 30% of US yoga users (Davies 2024). GUESS: stiff men are a group few studios talk to.
- Count your Rezerv bookings by gender, and who stops in month 1. No other Bangkok data exists.
- Try an occasional men's intro class. The 2024 study says regular ones "may not be necessary". A Danish centre had 2 men in a mixed class, 25 in a men-only one ([Copenhagen Post 2016](https://cphpost.dk/2016-10-21/news/men-only-yoga-classes-a-hit-in-denmark/), one story).
- Use "bring him along" invites. Men come when someone pushes.

**180K: gender barely matters. Don't chase men here.**
- GUESS: these barriers hit before a man starts. Your buyer started years ago. One small online survey found 17.5% men among regular Ashtanga practitioners, so most buyers are likely women, but that sample is too small to be sure. Sell depth, personal attention and getting unstuck to everyone.
- Helps both: FACT: only 12.2% of China's yoga teachers are men (2021). GUESS: a visible male teacher is rare and worth showing.

## 6. Value equation (Hormozi)

- **Dream outcome:** a body that moves without pain and keeps up with his sport. ("I didn't go for relaxation, for example." Jack, 40, Cagas 2024.)
- **Doubt:** "Too stiff, only man." Show stiff beginners and other men.
- **Time:** "When will I feel it?" Ask for a few sessions, not one (Cagas 2024).
- **Effort:** early mornings, a set sequence, flexible women around him. Shrink it: a guided first class, a friend beside him.

## 7. Inversion: how this could hurt AYBKK

- **Gym-bro tone loses the women.** GUESS, but women are the large majority in every source outside India (FACT). The 4 women in the 2024 study warned against "losing the true message and values of yoga".
- **"Manly yoga" gimmicks die.** FACT: broga.com is now a domain-for-sale page (checked 10 Oct 2026). One opinion piece said "broga" makes masculinity look "so fragile" ([UMSU 2015](https://umsu.unimelb.edu.au/news/article/7797/2015-02-23-the-trouble-with-broga)).
- **Selling challenge, then he gets stuck.** GUESS: a man who wants to be good straight away meets an asana he can't do yet, feels embarrassed, and quits. Always pair "hard work" with "stiff is normal".

## 8. Three content ideas

1. **Lane B: "Stiff is the start."** A stiff man's first week, filmed honestly. Men think yoga "primarily requires flexibility"; that idea "often dissipated" once they tried ([UP 2026](https://ovpaa.up.edu.ph/?p=7668)).
   Hook: "Can't touch your toes? That's not a reason to wait. It's the reason to start."
2. **Lane B: "Who brought you?"** A man and the partner or friend who pushed him in, one line each about week one.
   Hook: "Most men don't find yoga. Someone brings them."
3. **China workshops: "Yoga for your other training."** Ashtanga as support for lifting, running or fighting; breath and sweat, no chanting. Men score higher on yoga as support for another sport (2.74 vs 2.31 of 5, [Cagas 2022](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8776006/)).
   Hook: "你会撸铁，会跑步。不用手扶，你能盘腿坐下吗？" (You lift. You run. Can you sit cross-legged without your hands?)

## 9. Sources

- Wellcome Collection, 2018 (citing Singleton 2010): https://wellcomecollection.org/stories/yoga-adapts-to-time-and-place
- Birdee, J Gen Intern Med, 2008: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC2533368/
- Cagas, Complement Ther Clin Pract, 2020: https://www.qigonginstitute.org/abstract-print/16156
- Cagas, IJERPH, 2022: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8776006/
- Cagas, Am J Men's Health, 2024: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11580054/
- CDC NCHS, 2024: https://www.cdc.gov/nchs/products/databriefs/db501.htm
- Copenhagen Post, 2016: https://cphpost.dk/2016-10-21/news/men-only-yoga-classes-a-hit-in-denmark/
- Davies, Sci Rep, 2024: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11217305/
- Garnsey, J Bodyw Mov Ther, 2025: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jbmt.2025.01.055
- iResearch via Jiemian, 2018: https://www.jiemian.com/article/2438265.html
- Lifehacker.ru, 2020: https://lifehacker.ru/zanyatiya-jogoj-dlya-muzhchin/
- Mishra, Medicines, 2020: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7168227/
- Motzkus, Windsor thesis, 2022: https://uwindsor.scholaris.ca/server/api/core/bitstreams/4f7eb6d7-af7f-4fa9-9df8-78bcf2d2c731/content
- NNMS (ICMR-NCDIR), 2020: https://www.ncdirindia.org/nnms/resources/Chapter_4_6.pdf

## 2026-10-09 · plato-frogs-quiet-year

# Plato · 9 Oct 2026 · daily Reel package
Hidden Stories of Ashtanga (no Ep. number until filmed; last posted Ep. 4)
"The Frogs Who Kept Quiet for a Year"

Route: Lane A story with a Lane B door (the fear "I practise and nothing changes") · last 3: Vasishtha script (A, not filmed), Arjuna script (A, not filmed), doves script (A, not filmed) · next due: Lane B. The hook and the turn speak to beginners, so EN/TH captions carry the promo. Chinese stays Lane A (no promo).
Habit #1 Track everything (track the right thing: count your mornings, not the mirror). Also #2 Get stronger.

Trigger note: no unused life note, no @Plato request. News checked: UNICAMP (Brazil) study in Life Sciences, on ScienceDaily 8 Oct 2026 (via FAPESP). Obese mice did 7 strength sessions. Body weight did NOT change. But the fat around the gut went down and the fat cells got smaller. It is a mouse study (male mice only, one fat area). The script says "mice" and never says it works the same in people.

## The link (4 lines)
- Trigger: scientists trained mice for 7 days. The scale did not move. Inside, the fat cells were already getting smaller.
- Story: in the Rig Veda's Frog Hymn, the frogs lie quiet for a whole year "like Brahmans who fulfil their vows". When the rain comes, they all lift their voice together (Rig Veda 7.103.1).
- Behavior: the quiet season is not empty. It is a promise kept while nothing shows.
- Asana: Bhekasana (frog, Intermediate series). For months the feet do not come down. Same, same, same. The knees and thighs learn quietly. It trains keeping the promise before you see proof.

Toolkit pick: Myth-Kill ("nothing shows = nothing happens". No. The fact.) + Root and Wobble (name the awkward middle as a stage: "the quiet season") inside the /story beats. Why: the study IS a myth-kill in one line (scale same, inside changed), and the frog year gives the viewer a name for the weeks where they want to quit. Not used in the last 3 days (8 Oct /hook + Vibe Samurai, 7 Oct Triple Hook, 6 Oct One Dissenting Voice).
Other options: (1) Name-the-Feeling: "That voice in week three, 'it's not working'... I know it." (2) Gervais close on himself: "Twenty years. My Bhekasana still... is having its quiet year." (only if he likes it).

## Hooks (/hook, answered myself)
Topic: practice habit, quitting in the weeks when nothing shows.
Consensus: "If you don't see results in a month, change the program."
Where Boonchu sharpens it: 20 years of Ashtanga, six days a week since 2008. Change in the body comes before you can see it; the job is to keep coming.
1. Authority: "Twenty years on the mat. Most of the change, nobody saw." (his claim: OK it first)
2. Contrarian: "The mirror is the worst teacher you will ever have."
3. Stakes: "Quit in the quiet weeks, and you quit while it's working."
4. Question: "If nothing changes outside, is anything changing inside?"
5. Pattern interrupt: "Scientists trained mice for 7 days. The scale? Same."
Pick: 5. It opens with the trigger, a 10-year-old gets it, and it sets a trap ("so it failed?") that the next line snaps ("inside, it already changed"). Hook 2 goes on the cover.

## Script (60 s, spoken to camera, his spoken English)
HOOK (0-5 s)
"This week, scientists train mice. Seven days. The scale? ... Same.
But inside... the fat cells already getting smaller."
[card: mouse puppet on a tiny scale, needle not moving] [zoom punch: "Same."]

CONTEXT + MIRROR (5-13 s)
"Maybe this is you. One month on the mat. You look in the mirror... nothing.
And a small voice say: it's not working. Stop."
[card: mirror, ❌ "nothing"]

ORDEAL: THE STORY, bedtime voice (13-38 s)
"At night I tell my son a story. From a very old book, the Rig Veda.
In the dry time, the frogs go quiet. One whole year. No song.
Like a monk keeping a promise.
People walk by the dry pond and say: nothing living here.
But the frogs are there. Under the mud. Keeping the promise.
Then one day... the rain come.
And all the frogs... sing. Together."
[silent beat, 2 s, rain sound]
"The quiet year was not empty. It was the promise."

TAKEAWAY: THE TURN + ASANA (38-55 s)
"In Ashtanga we also have a frog. Bhekasana.
You lie on your belly. You press your feet down, next to your hips.
[BOONCHU'S OWN CUE GOES HERE: what he tells a student in Bhekasana, 1-2 lines]
For many months, the feet don't come down. Same, same, same.
But the knees, the thighs... they are learning. Quietly."

RETURN / PAYOFF (55-60 s)
"So don't ask the mirror. Ask: did I come today?
Mice, frogs, you... the change start inside first."

Payoff check: the hook opens "scale same, so did it fail?"; the last line closes it ("the change starts inside first"). One lesson only: keep the promise in the quiet season.

Verify before filming:
- His Bhekasana cue in his own words (Plato did not write one).
- Hook 1 claim, if used.
- Say "mice" every time. Never "this works for people" or any kilo or weight-loss promise.
- Bhekasana is Intermediate series: if he wants a Primary link for beginners, say "in Ashtanga, the frog comes later; the promise starts on day one".

## Edit cue sheet (for the edit session)
- Header pill: "Hidden Stories of Ashtanga · Ep. N" (N = last posted + 1 when filmed).
- Zoom punches 1.3x on: "Same.", "inside", "nothing", "one whole year", "sing", "promise", "Bhekasana", "did I come today". SFX at 40%, 0.4 s after the word: click-soft (Same), whoosh (inside), error (nothing), chime (sing), soft rain + low thunder under the silent beat.
- Captions: Weight Shift, big emoji: 🐭 ⚖️ 🪞 🐸 🌧️ 🎶 🤞
- Cards (Isle of Dogs stop-motion prompts, keep the handmade cues):
  1. "stop-motion puppet animation film still in the style of Wes Anderson's Isle of Dogs: a handmade tabletop diorama, a small felt mouse with glassy bead eyes standing on a tiny brass kitchen scale, the needle at the same mark, painted cardboard lab wall, flat front-on camera, centered symmetrical tableau, visible fibers and handmade imperfections"
  2. "... a dry cracked clay pond in a handmade tabletop diorama, two felt frogs, one green and one spotty, sitting very still half in the mud, eyes closed, painted cardboard sky, cotton-wool clouds on visible wires, warm dusk light ..." (verse 6 names a green frog and a spotty one)
  3. "... the same pond full of cut-felt water, rain made of glass beads on threads, many felt frogs with mouths open singing together, cotton-wool rain clouds on visible wires ..."
- No god's face needed (Parjanya, the rain, is only rain).

## Sources (check before filming)
- News: ScienceDaily, 8 Oct 2026, "Just 7 Days of Strength Training Started Shrinking Fat Cells in Mice" https://www.sciencedaily.com/releases/2026/10/261006234509.htm (UNICAMP, Life Sciences vol. 401, 124546; male mice, mesenteric fat, body weight unchanged).

# Plato · 8 Oct 2026 · daily Reel package
Hidden Stories of Ashtanga (no Ep. number until filmed; last posted Ep. 4)
"The Two Stars Who Walk Together"

Route: Lane A story with a Lane B door (couples / "bring someone" fits the DUO promo: bring a friend) · last 3: Arjuna script (A, not filmed), doves script (A, not filmed), carousels 7 Oct · next due: Lane B fear buster. This one invites beginners in pairs, so it leans B in EN/TH.
Habit #3 Partners on the same path (and #9 Do it with others).

Trigger note: no unused life note. News checked: BYU study, published by ScienceDaily 6 Oct 2026. Nearly 1,700 young married couples. Couples who exercise together REPORT better talking, better fixing fights, less stress. It is a link, not proof of cause, and the effect is small: the script says "say", never "makes".

## The link (4 lines)
- Trigger: scientists asked almost 1,700 married couples. The ones who exercise together say they talk better and fight less.
- Story: the sage Vasishtha and his wife Arundhati live on as two stars side by side in the seven sages (the Big Dipper). At old Indian weddings the new husband shows his bride that star (Ashvalayana Grihya Sutra 1.7.22; Mahabharata, Adi Parva 235).
- Behavior: they are not one strong star. They are two stars on the same road, every night.
- Asana: Vasisthasana (Advanced A). Your whole body on one hand, one straight line. Alone it shakes. Next to someone who shows up, you show up too.

Toolkit pick: /hook (pattern interrupt) + Vibe Samurai ladder inside the /story beats (ladder: the couples in the study / Vasishtha and Arundhati / you and the person on the next mat). Why: the study gives a plain, surprising opener anyone gets ("fight less"), and the ladder walks it from strangers to the sky to the viewer's own home. Not used in the last 3 days (Name-the-Feeling, One Dissenting Voice, Triple Hook).
Other options: (1) Gervais close: "My wife and I, we practise in the same room. We still argue. But... less." (only if true). (2) Fork-First: "6 am. Your partner is still asleep. Do you go alone, or wake them up?"

## Hooks (/hook, answered myself)
Topic: practice habit, why people quit alone.
Consensus: "Exercise is a personal thing. Your own time, your own goals."
Where Boonchu sharpens it: 20 years of practice, six days a week since 2008, in a shala where people practise side by side every morning. People who come with someone keep coming (his own view: say it only if he agrees).
1. Authority: "Twenty years in a shala. The ones who stay rarely come alone." (his claim: OK it first)
2. Contrarian: "Your workout is not 'me time'. That is why you quit."
3. Stakes: "Train alone, and you come home with nothing to say."
4. Question: "Why do couples who move together fight less?"
5. Pattern interrupt: "Scientists asked 1,700 couples. The ones who exercise together... fight less."
Pick: #5. It opens with the trigger (rule), "fight less" lands with sound off and makes a married 10-year practitioner look at the person next to them. The number is checked (BYU, nearly 1,700).

## Script (about 60 s, spoken to camera, his English)
HOOK (0-4 s)
"Scientists ask almost 1,700 married couples.
The ones who exercise together... they say they fight less."

CONTEXT + MIRROR (4-12 s)
"Maybe you go to the gym. Your partner stay home.
Or you both go... different place, different time.
Night, you come home. Tired. Nothing to say."

STORY, bedtime voice (12-34 s)
"Long time ago in India, there is a sage. Vasishtha.
His wife, Arundhati.
The old books say... now they live in the sky. Two stars.
Look at the seven big stars, the Big Dipper.
In the handle... one bright star. And right next to it... a tiny one.
(mirror) Small star, big star. Side by side.
... (silent beat, 1 s)
And in old Indian weddings, at night, the husband take his new wife outside.
And he show her... that tiny star."

TURN (34-42 s)
"Not because one star is strong.
Because every night... they walk the same road."

TAKEAWAY, the reveal (42-54 s)
"In Ashtanga we have Vasisthasana. His asana.
Your whole body on one hand. One straight line.
Alone... it shake.
But in the shala, the person on the next mat is breathing with you.
They come... you come."

RETURN, payoff (54-60 s)
"You want to fight less at home?
Practise together. Same time. Same room."

Payoff check: the hook opens "why do they fight less?"; the close answers it in the viewer's own home. One ordeal (doing it alone, coming home empty), one lesson (move together and you keep going).

- Zoom punch + SFX at 40% (0.4 s after the word): "1,700" (ping), "fight less" (impact-bass-1), "nothing to say" (click-soft), "two stars" (sparkle), "tiny one" (chime), "wedding" (chime), "same road" (whoosh), "one hand" (pop), "shake" (error), "together" (sparkle).
- Cards: (1) graphic card "👫 1,700 couples · move together → talk more, fight less", "say" in small text under it; (2) graphic: the Big Dipper, the handle star glows, a tiny star pops in next to it (gold), labels "Vasishtha" and "Arundhati"; (3) the sage and his wife walking side by side; (4) a wedding couple at night looking up, his hand pointing; (5) graphic "Vasisthasana = Vasishtha's asana · one hand, one line"; (6) a real AYBKK class photo of two mats side by side, only if the students gave permission; (7) last card "Same time. Same room. ✨✨".
- Picture prompts (Isle of Dogs, keep every handmade cue): "stop-motion puppet animation film still in the style of Wes Anderson's Isle of Dogs: a handmade tabletop diorama, [SCENE], felt and wool puppets with glassy bead eyes, painted cardboard sky, cotton-wool clouds on visible wires, flat front-on camera, centered symmetrical tableau, visible fibers and handmade imperfections". Scenes: (1) an old felt sage with a long white wool beard and his wife in a simple felt sari walking side by side on a forest path at dusk; (2) a painted cardboard night sky with seven tin-foil stars in the Big Dipper shape, one bright star in the handle and a tiny star right next to it, both hanging on visible wires; (3) a young felt bride and groom in simple wedding clothes standing outside a cardboard house at night, he points up at the two stars, a tiny clay lamp at their feet. No gods' faces. Cloudflare only after Boonchu's OK.

## Sources (check before filming)
- News: ScienceDaily, 6 Oct 2026, "Scientists Say This Simple Habit Could Strengthen Your Marriage" (Brigham Young University). https://www.sciencedaily.com/releases/2026/10/261004001843.htm . Study: Yorgason, Mildenhall, Nunez, "Conjoint Exercise among young married couples...", Family Relations 2026, DOI 10.1111/fare.70260. Nearly 1,700 couples (CREATE study). About 60% exercised, only about a third together; walking was the most common shared exercise. Lead author: "While the impact is not necessarily large, it is very consistent." Survey link, not cause.
- Story 1: Ashvalayana Grihya Sutra 1.7.22, tr. Oldenberg, Sacred Books of the East vol. 29, p. 170: "When she sees the polar-star, the star Arundhati, and the seven Rishis (ursa major), let her break the silence" and say "May my husband live and I get offspring." (Also Paraskara Grihya Sutra 1.8.19: he shows her the polar star after sunset.)
- Story 2: Mahabharata, tr. Ganguli, Adi Parva sec. 235 (checked on Gutenberg 15474): Arundhati "has become a little star... (amongst a constellation of seven bright stars representing the seven Rishis)". Vana Parva sec. 225: Swaha could take the form of the other six sages' wives but not Arundhati's, "on account of the great ascetic merit of Arundhati and her devotion to her husband (Vasishtha)".
- Sky: Vasishtha = Mizar, Arundhati = Alcor, the naked-eye pair in the Big Dipper's handle (Chinese: 开阳 and 辅). Standard Indian sky lore; check with a star chart before filming.
- Asana: Vasisthasana is in the Advanced A series.

## Flags for Boonchu
- LEFT OUT on purpose: the Adi Parva passage says Arundhati became a dim star BECAUSE she was jealous of Vasishtha, and the speaker adds "men should never trust women". Too risky (looks sexist, blames the wife). The script uses only the stars side by side and the wedding custom.
- Vasishtha was in the 30 Sep story as the one who recites Rama's line (Marichyasana). Different story and asana.
- Hook #1 and the Gervais close are your own claims: use only if true for you.
- Your Vasisthasana cue: 1 or 2 lines in your own words would beat "one straight line".
- Promo line in EN/TH only (the "bring a friend" DUO offer fits this Reel well). Chinese and Russian: invite, no promo, no price.

## 2026-10-07 · plato-hand-in-the-dark

# Plato · 7 Oct 2026 · daily Reel package
Hidden Stories of Ashtanga (no Ep. number until filmed; last posted Ep. 4)
"The Hand That Knew the Way"

Route: Lane A (Hidden Stories), beginner-safe, Habit Gap message · last 3: doves script (A, not filmed), hen script (A, not filmed), carousels 5 Oct · next due: a Lane B fear buster (this one stays open-door: "nobody does it on day one").
Habit #1 Track everything: count the mornings, not the wins (the 12-week check).
Trigger note: no unused life note. News checked: a golfer won his first PGA Tour title on his 100th start, Sun 4 Oct 2026 (US time). Plain enough for a 10-year-old: "100th try". No score, no tour name, no golfer name in the spoken script.

## The link (4 lines)
- Trigger: this week a golfer won his first big title. On his 100th try. 99 times before, no win.
- Story: young Arjuna is eating dinner. The wind blows out the lamp. He keeps eating in the dark: his hand finds his mouth "from habit". So he starts to practise his bow at night too (Mahabharata, Adi Parva, section 134, Ganguli).
- Behavior: the 99 tries were not nothing. They were the dinners in the dark. Habit teaches the body a way you cannot see yet.
- Asana: Urdhva Dhanurasana, the upward bow, and the drop back. Standing, you bend back to a floor you cannot see. Nobody does it on day one. One morning, the hands just find the floor.

Toolkit pick: Triple Hook (Proof + Whiplash) + /story beats, fail-hero-payoff (fail = "nothing changes, I quit at week three"; hero = Arjuna's hand, then the practitioner; payoff = "count the mornings"). Why: "100th try" is a number anyone gets in one second, and the whiplash ("the 99 were not failures") flips what the viewer believes about his own slow progress. Not used in the last 3 days (Name-the-Feeling 5 Oct, One Dissenting Voice 6 Oct).
Other options: (1) Gervais close: "Me? I needed more than 100 tries for my drop back. Don't ask how many." (only if true for him). (2) Fork-First: "Week three. No change. Do you quit, or do you eat in the dark?"

## Hooks (/hook, answered myself)
Topic: practice habit, why people quit before it works.
Consensus: "If you see no progress, the method is wrong for you. Try something else."
Where Boonchu sharpens it: 20 years of Ashtanga, six days a week since 2008. Progress hides inside the habit until one morning it shows. The drop back is the classic: months of nothing, then one day the hands land.
1. Authority: "Twenty years of Ashtanga. Most progress I never saw coming." (his own claim: OK it first)
2. Contrarian: "No progress for weeks? That is not a sign to stop."
3. Stakes: "Quit at try 99, and you never meet try 100."
4. Question: "What were the 99 tries before the win doing?"
5. Pattern interrupt: "This week a golfer won on his 100th try. The 99 before were not failures."
Pick: #5. It opens with the trigger (rule), the number makes sense with sound off, and it turns the viewer's own "I failed" into "I was building". A 10-year practitioner stuck on a drop back stops for that.

## Script (60 s, spoken to camera, his English)

HOOK (0-4 s)
"This week, a golfer win his first big title. On his 100th try.
The 99 before... not failures."

CONTEXT + MIRROR (4-12 s)
"You practise three weeks. Nothing change.
You think: maybe I'm not made for this.
Okay. Listen this story."

STORY, bedtime voice (12-32 s)
"Long time ago in India. A boy, Arjuna. He is learning the bow.
One night he eat dinner. Wind come... whoosh. The lamp go out.
All dark.
But his hand... still go to his mouth. Every time.
(mirror) He cannot see. But his hand know the way.
The old book say: from habit.
... (silent beat, 1 s)
So Arjuna think: if my hand can eat in the dark... my bow can shoot in the dark.
And he start to practise. At night."

ORDEAL / TURN (32-42 s)
"The 99 tries... they are the dinners in the dark.
You don't see anything. But your body is learning the way."

TAKEAWAY, the reveal (42-54 s)
"In Ashtanga we have Urdhva Dhanurasana. Upward bow.
Standing, you bend back... to the floor. You cannot see it.
Nobody do it on day one.
Then one morning... the hands just find the floor."

RETURN, payoff (54-60 s)
"So don't count the wins.
Count the mornings."

Payoff check: the hook says "the 99 before were not failures"; the close "count the mornings" tells the viewer what the 99 were for. One ordeal (the dark), one lesson (habit builds what you cannot see yet).

## 2026-10-06 · plato-doves-net

# Plato · 6 Oct 2026 · daily Reel package · v2 KID VERSION (rewritten 11 Oct)
Hidden Stories of Ashtanga (no Ep. number until filmed; last posted Ep. 4)
"The Doves Who Lifted the Net"

Boonchu, 11 Oct: "rewrite every piece to be talking like 10 years old or telling story to 10 year old kids."
How: 3 writers (faithful / bedtime storyteller / kid talk), 3 judges each (a 10-year-old who learns English, Boonchu as a 53-year-old viewer, facts and rules), 1 editor, 4 caption writers with 2 checks each, then a fresh child reader and a rules sweep. 31 agents. Winner: "faithful" 22.5/30, with lines grafted from the other two. Plato merged the last fixes by hand.
SOURCE FIX: v1 said "every dove pull, pull, pull, alone". That is NOT in the book. In Ryder the doves are just scared in the net; the leader says don't be afraid, fly up together. v2 removes it.

Route: Lane A (Hidden Stories), beginner-safe · Habit #9 Do it with others.
Point (one line a child can repeat): One dove can't lift the net. Together, they can.

## The link (4 lines)
- Trigger: Yoga is easy in class. At home alone, you say "tomorrow."
- Story: Doves get stuck in a net. They are scared. All together, they fly up with the net. A mouse friend sets them free.
- Behavior: One dove can't lift the net. Together, they can. You're not lazy. You're one dove.
- Asana: Kapotasana is the yoga dove, a big bend back. Scary alone. Less scary when people breathe with you.

Toolkit pick: One Dissenting Voice (hook: "You're not lazy") + /story beats, told as a bedtime story, then "Okay, big people" turns to the adults.

## Hooks (all under 12 words)
1. Authority: "Hard alone at home? Twenty years of yoga. I know why." (needs your OK)
2. Contrarian: "Mat still rolled up? You think, 'I'm lazy.' You're not." ← PICK
3. Stakes: "Every 'tomorrow' makes the mat heavier. Not your fault."
4. Question: "Why is yoga easy in class, but so hard at home?"
5. Pattern interrupt: "Your rolled-up mat at home? Some birds know why."
Why #2: it says the viewer's own inner voice ("I'm lazy"), says no, and saves "together" for the end.

## Script (about 60 s, your spoken English, 158 words + your cue)
HOOK (0-4 s) [close, to camera, plain voice. A rolled-up mat on the floor in the frame]
"Mat still rolled up? You think, 'I'm lazy.' No... you're not."

MIRROR (4-8 s)
"In yoga class... easy. At home, alone... 'Tomorrow.' 'Tomorrow.'"

STORY (8-36 s) [softer, slower, bedtime voice. Lean in a little]
"Bedtime story...
A man want to catch birds. He put a net, some rice... and hide."
[real doves on screen]
"Big dove and many doves see the rice.
They fly down... oh no! Stuck in the net."
[quick look to camera] "Like you... stuck in 'tomorrow.'"
[bedtime voice] "Doves so scared."
[quick look to camera] "You too... alone at home?"
[bedtime voice] "Big dove say..." [soft, half to camera] "'Don't be scared.'"
[SILENT BEAT, 2 s: hold eye contact, hold the picture of the doves in the net]
"'All together... fly!'
They fly up... with the net! No more scared.
A mouse friend chew the net. Free!"

TURN (36-42 s) [1 s silence. Sit up, normal voice, now to the grown-ups]
"Okay, big people. One dove can't lift the net. Together, they can.
You're not lazy... you're one dove."

ASANA (42-56 s) [film this in the morning room: many mats, same time. You in Kapotasana with people around you, not alone]
"In the old language of India, dove is kapota.
Kapotasana... the yoga dove.
You bend back." [touch your heel] "Hands look for your feet... you cannot see them.
Scary, like the doves."
[soft voice, like the big dove] [BOONCHU'S OWN KAPOTASANA CUE, 5 words or less]
"With people breathing with you... less scary."

PAYOFF (56-60 s) [small smile, unroll the same mat from the hook]
"Find your doves. Same time, every day."

Loop: the hook shows a rolled-up mat; the payoff unrolls it. The hook says "you're not lazy"; the turn says why: you're one dove.

- 48 s: Hands find your feet. You can't see them.

(Move the times to match your real take.)

## Edit cue sheet
- Zoom punch + SFX at 40%, 0.4 s after the word: "lazy" (error), "net" (whoosh), "oh no" (impact-bass), "scared" (click-soft), "fly!" (whoosh), "together" (chime), "kapota" (sparkle), "doves" in the payoff (ping).
- Picture prompts (Isle of Dogs, keep every handmade cue): "stop-motion puppet animation film still in the style of Wes Anderson's Isle of Dogs: a handmade tabletop diorama, [SCENE], felt and wool puppets with glassy bead eyes, painted cardboard sky, cotton-wool clouds on visible wires, flat front-on camera, centered symmetrical tableau, visible fibers and handmade imperfections". Scenes: (1) a man in felt clothes hiding behind a cardboard bush, a string net and rice grains on the ground; (2) grey felt doves landing on the rice; (3) the doves huddled under the string net, scared bead eyes (NOT pulling apart: that was invented); (4) the whole flock flying up together, carrying the net like a canopy over cardboard hills; (5) a small felt mouse chewing the string net. No club, no harm. Cloudflare only after your OK.

ZH (Xiaohongshu / WeChat Channels; Lane A: no promo, no price)
【阿斯汤加的隐藏故事】
瑜伽垫还卷着？你觉得是自己太懒？其实不是。
在课上，一下子就练起来了。一个人在家，就总是说：“明天吧……明天吧……”
印度有个很老的动物故事：一群鸽子看见地上有米，飞下来吃，哎呀，一下子被地上的大网缠住了！它们好害怕。领头的大鸽子说：“别怕，大家一起飞！”
大家一起用力，呼啦一下，带着网飞上了天！它们一下子就不怕了。它们飞去找老鼠朋友，老鼠把网一点一点咬开，鸽子们都钻出来啦！
鸽子们没有突然变厉害，它们是一起用力，才飞起来的。所以你不懒，你只是一只自己飞的鸽子。一只鸽子，可拉不动一张网呀。
在印度一种很老很老的语言里，鸽子叫 kapota。Kapotasana（鸽子式）是一个往后弯腰的体式。手要往后去找自己的脚，可是眼睛看不见，有点吓人，就像网里的鸽子。有人陪你一起呼吸，就没那么怕了。慢慢来，不着急。
去找你的鸽子们吧：一个朋友、一个瑜伽班，或者网上的小伙伴。每天同一个时间，一起练。
如果你来曼谷，欢迎到 AYBKK 的迈索尔教室练一练（大家在一个房间里一起练，老师就在旁边），新手也欢迎哦。想知道怎么来，就看看我的主页吧。
#阿斯汤加的隐藏故事 #阿斯汤加 #迈索尔 #鸽子式 #印度故事

Xiaohongshu title (13 characters): 瑜伽垫还卷着？其实你不懒！

RU (invite only, no promo)
Скрытые истории аштанги · Голуби, которые подняли сеть
Коврик дома всё ещё свёрнут? Думаешь, это лень? А вот и нет.
В зале заниматься легко. А дома в одиночку всё «завтра да завтра»...
Вот индийская сказка. Голуби слетелись поклевать рис и попали в сеть! Страшно!
Главный голубь сказал: «Не бойтесь! Взлетаем все вместе!»
И они унесли сеть по небу к своему другу мышонку. А мышонок её перегрыз. Ура, спасены!
На древнем языке Индии «капота» значит «голубь». Капотасана значит «асана голубя».
Ты прогибаешься назад, руки ищут пятки, а пяток не видно. Страшно, как тем голубям в сети! Но когда другие дышат вместе с тобой, уже не так страшно.
Голубей спасла не сила. Их спасло то, что они были вместе.
Ты не ленишься. Ты просто голубь без стаи. Найди свою стаю: друга, зал или группу. Каждый день в одно и то же время.
Приходи к нам заниматься на майсор в AYBKK, в Бангкоке. Майсор: все занимаются в одной комнате, а учитель рядом. Новичкам рады: научим всему с самого начала.

## Sources (check before filming)
- The Panchatantra, tr. Arthur W. Ryder (1925), Book II "The Winning of Friends", frame story. The leader (Ryder: Gay-Neck) and his doves land in a hunter's snare; they are "distressed"; he says "We must all agree in purpose, must fly up in unison, and carry the snare away"; they fly "just an arrow-shot into the air", "proceeded without fear"; the hunter gives up; the mouse Gold chews them free. Text: https://theoceanofstories.blogspot.com/2013/01/panchatantra-winning-of-friends.html
- Left out on purpose: a crow warned the leader and he did not listen (he led them into the net); the Bharunda bird story (a bird dies); the hunter's club; "king of the doves"; the leader asking the mouse to free his doves first (a second lesson).
- kapota = dove, pigeon (Monier-Williams). The script says "dove is kapota", never "the story says kapota".

## 2026-10-05 · plato-hen-eggs

# Plato · 5 Oct 2026 · daily Reel package
Hidden Stories of Ashtanga (no Ep. number until filmed; last posted Ep. 4, Ep. 5 = Foolish Old Man script)
"The Hen Who Did Not Check"

Route: Lane A (Hidden Stories) with a Habit Gap message, beginner-safe · last 3: Flex vs Stiff (B), adjustment Reel (A), carousels Mountain / Hard Way · Habit #1 Track everything (honest version: check at week 0, 6, 12, not every morning).
Trigger note: no strong safe news in the last 3 days (Twin Cities Marathon, ASICS survey 29 Sep, diet-drink trial: all weak or old). Standing order PLAIN TRIGGER says: if the news link is weak, use the viewer's own body. So the trigger is the viewer checking for change every morning.

## The link (4 lines)
- Trigger: you check your body every morning. No change. Week three, you stop.
- Story: the Buddha's two hens. One wishes her chicks would come out but does not sit on the eggs. One just sits, keeps them warm, does not even wish, and the chicks break out by themselves (Samyutta Nikaya 22.101).
- Behavior: wishing and checking do not hatch anything. Sitting does.
- Asana: Garbha Pindasana (the embryo, you roll like an egg) into Kukkutasana (the rooster, you come up on your hands). Pali for hen = kukkuti. Same word.

Toolkit pick: Name-the-Feeling + /story beats, with a Root and Wobble reveal (kukkuti / Kukkutasana). Why: the viewer's own habit (checking, then quitting) is the hook, and the old word links the hen to the asana without any outside knowledge.
Other options: (1) Triple Hook (Contradiction): "Everyone says track your progress. A hen says no. She's right." (2) Gervais deadpan: "My fitness app has a streak. The hen has chicks."

## Hooks (/hook, answered myself)
Topic: practice habit, why people quit in the first weeks.
Consensus: "Track your progress every day so you stay motivated."
Where Boonchu sharpens it: 20 years of Ashtanga (AYBKK open since 2013, per aybkk.net). Daily checking makes people quit when the change is slow. Check at set times (week 0, 6, 12), sit on the eggs in between.
1. Authority: "Twenty years of Ashtanga. The students who stay stopped checking." (fixed 5 Oct per Machi: never "fourteen"; the site says the shala opened in 2013)
2. Contrarian: "You don't quit because it's hard. You quit because you keep checking."
3. Stakes: "Week three. No change. This is the morning you stay home."
4. Question: "Do you look in the mirror after practice, for any change?"
5. Pattern interrupt: "A chicken knows more about practice than your fitness app."
PICK: #2. Why: 11 words, names the viewer's own habit (mirror rule), flips what they believe (it's not the hard part), opens a loop the hen closes. Works sound-off as on-screen text. #1 needs Boonchu's OK (his own claim).

## Script (/story, 60 s, spoken to camera, his spoken English)
One ordeal: checking instead of sitting. One lesson: sit on your eggs.

HOOK (0-4 s)
"You don't quit because it's hard. You quit because you keep checking."

CONTEXT + MIRROR (4-13 s)
"Every morning. Mirror. 'Any change?' ... Nothing.
Week three... still nothing. So tomorrow... you stay home.
I know. Everybody do this."

STORY, bedtime voice (13-33 s)
"The Buddha tell a small story. Two hens. Each one, eight, ten, twelve eggs.
First hen... she wish. 'Come out, my chicks! Come out!'
But she don't sit on the eggs. Eggs don't stay warm.
Nothing come out.
(mirror) That is us. Wishing in front of the mirror.
Second hen... she don't wish anything. She just sit. Keep them warm.
... (silent beat, 1 s)
And one day... crack. The chicks come out. By themself."

ORDEAL / TURN (33-43 s)
"Wishing don't hatch the egg. Checking don't hatch the egg.
Sitting... hatch the egg."

TAKEAWAY, the reveal (43-56 s)
"In old Pali, hen is kukkuti. In Ashtanga we have Kukkutasana. The rooster.
Before it, Garbha Pindasana. Garbha pinda mean the embryo. You roll, like an egg.
Nobody jump into Kukkutasana. You roll, every morning... and one day, you come up on your hands."

RETURN, payoff (56-62 s)
"So... stop checking every day. Check at week zero, week six, week twelve.
In between? Sit on your eggs."

Payoff: the hook said "you keep checking"; the last line tells you when to check and what to do in between. Loop closed.

## Edit cues (house style)
- Header pill: "Hidden Stories of Ashtanga · Ep. N" (number only when filmed).
- Zoom punch 1.3x + SFX at 40%, 0.4 s after: "checking" (click-soft), "Nothing" (error), "crack" (pop), "kukkuti" (sparkle), "Kukkutasana" (chime), "Sit on your eggs" (impact-bass-1).
- Graphic card on the turn: ❌ wishing / ❌ checking / ✅ sitting, item by item.
- Card on the reveal: "kukkuti (hen) → Kukkutasana (rooster)" gold on navy.
- Keep the 1 s silence before "crack".
- Show a real student rolling in Garbha Pindasana and lifting into Kukkutasana (only with permission; never a student failing as the "problem" shot).

Picture prompts (Isle of Dogs, keep all handmade cues):
1. "stop-motion puppet animation film still in the style of Wes Anderson's Isle of Dogs: a handmade tabletop diorama, a felt and wool hen with glassy bead eyes standing up and staring anxiously at a nest of twelve eggs in a straw nest, painted cardboard sky, cotton-wool clouds on visible wires, flat front-on camera, centered symmetrical tableau, visible fibers and handmade imperfections"
2. Same style: "a calm felt hen sitting low over her nest, eyes half closed, warm lantern light, quiet village courtyard of painted cardboard"
3. Same style: "tiny felt chicks breaking out of cracked eggshells with their beaks under the sitting hen, cut-paper shell pieces, soft morning light"
- Do NOT picture the Buddha (no statue, no face, no figure). Show only the hens. A Buddha image in a casual edit can upset viewers in Thailand.

## 2026-10-04 · plato-chat-letter-01-draft

# Letter from the Shala #1 (APPROVED English core, Boonchu 'go' 4 Oct) · Plato · 4 Oct 2026

Skeleton: Halbert Boron 1, "the first letter of a series" (bots/notebook/halbert-letter.md, Issue 1).
Direction: Habit Gap core ("same practice, same time, a teacher who notices"). Habit numbers: #1 Track (count your practice days). Sleep (#4) not covered this week.
Send: MONDAY (Boonchu, 4 Oct: Monday letters motivate people to come and practice). Handwritten by Boonchu with a fountain pen, photo posted with the typed text. LINE OA (Thai, Lane B P.S.), WeChat OA article boonchuaybkk (Chinese, Lane A P.S.), Telegram 3 groups (Russian, Lane A P.S.).
Rules kept from Carne's #1: no price, no program name, no "apply", no "authorized". Trust first. (The LINE promo line keeps Boonchu's own Sharathji honour words, as ordered on 3 Oct.)

---

**Subject:** Why I'm going to write to you every week

[Day], [time]. [Where you are writing.]

Dear friend,

This is the first of many letters. Every week, I will write you one thing that 20 years of Ashtanga have taught me. For 18 of those years, I have practiced six days a week.

Practice. Breath. Rest. Getting up early. Being stuck. One letter a week. Three minutes to read.

These letters won't be polished. I would rather write to you every week than write one perfect letter a year.

So let me start with a student who has practiced with me for nearly 10 years.

A few months ago, she started to do both legs behind her head. Dwi Pada Sirsasana. It did not feel comfortable right away. She asked me, "Is it ever going to get better?" I said, "It will... with your patience."

Legs behind the head takes time. Some people need 2 or 3 years just to start to feel comfortable. Some need much longer, and they start to feel there is no way. Maybe you know that feeling, from an asana you are dealing with now.

She did not stop her routine. And recently, I can feel it getting much easier for her.

How do you keep going when it feels like there is no way?

That brings me to the one habit I would give you, if I could give you only one:

**Practice at the same time every morning.**

Alarm. Tea or coffee. Nauli, rolling the belly muscles. Mat. That's it.

You don't need more motivation. You need fewer decisions, and a teacher who notices and asks when you are not in the shala. When the time is fixed, there is nothing left to decide.

If it sounds like I am making a big deal out of a small thing, I am. I have not found anything that matters more.

By the way, "every morning" means every practice day, not Saturdays or moon days. And it does not mean the full series. On a bad day, a few Sun Salutations count. The time is the habit. The length can change.

**This week, try this:** pick one time. Be on your mat at that time on your next 3 practice days. That's all.

See you on the mat,
Boonchu

P.S. Remember, we are all growing "old", and we want to keep our energy and strength for a long time. (I wish we could say we are growing young.)

P.P.S. Next Monday: "Am I flexible enough?" My honest answer.

TYPED UNDER THE PHOTO (LINE, Thai): New to Mysore? Special promotion for new students: buy 1, get 1 free on 1, 2 or 3 months. Bring a friend, or keep both for yourself. Buy by 11 Nov. (In honour of Sharathji's passing.) Ask us here on LINE @aybkk.

TYPED UNDER THE PHOTO (WeChat): One small favor. Send this account a message with two things: your city, and how many years you have practiced. One line is enough.

TYPED UNDER THE PHOTO (Telegram): One small favor. Message me at @Boonchutanti with two things: your city, and how many years you have practiced. One line is enough.


---

## WeChat-only extra (optional, Chinese readers know this story)
Put it after "How do you keep going when it feels like there is no way?":
"There is an old Chinese story. A man, nearly 90 years old, wanted to move the mountains in front of his house. He carried the earth away, one basket at a time. A wise old man laughed at him. He answered: when I die, my sons and grandsons will keep going. The mountains will not grow. In the end, the god of the sky had the mountains carried away." (Liezi, 愚公移山. Never political, no wordplay on the title, 天帝 for the god.)

3. Teaching lines CONFIRMED by Boonchu 4 Oct (keep both). Routine CONFIRMED: alarm, tea or coffee, nauli, mat. Student story rewritten in his words (many years; started Dwi Pada a few months ago; asked if it gets better; 'It will... with your patience'; recently much easier).
4. Telegram: @Boonchutanti (confirmed).

## Language notes
- Thai (LINE): local dateline (the street, the rain, the sound). Mysore = มายซอร์. Asana = อาสนะ.
- Chinese (WeChat article): asana = 体式. A native reader checks it out loud.
- Russian (Telegram): asana = асана. A native reader checks it out loud.
- The student: no name, no photo unless she says yes.

## Thai · LINE OA
```
Subject: ทำไมถึงจะเขียนหาคุณทุกสัปดาห์

[วัน] [เวลา] [ตอนนี้นั่งเขียนอยู่ที่ไหน]

เพื่อนที่รัก

นี่คือจดหมายฉบับแรก และจะมีอีกหลายฉบับตามมา ทุกสัปดาห์จะเขียนเล่าให้ฟังหนึ่งเรื่อง ที่ได้เรียนรู้จากการฝึกอัษฎางคะมา 20 ปี ใน 20 ปีนั้น มี 18 ปีที่ฝึกสัปดาห์ละ 6 วัน

การฝึก ลมหายใจ การพักผ่อน การตื่นเช้า ช่วงที่ย่ำอยู่กับที่
สัปดาห์ละฉบับ อ่านจบใน 3 นาที

จดหมายพวกนี้จะไม่เนี้ยบหรอก เราขอเขียนถึงคุณทุกสัปดาห์ ดีกว่าเขียนจดหมายที่สมบูรณ์แบบปีละฉบับ

งั้นขอเริ่มด้วยเรื่องของนักเรียนคนหนึ่ง ที่ฝึกกับเรามาเกือบ 10 ปี

เมื่อไม่กี่เดือนก่อน เธอเริ่มเอาขาทั้งสองข้างไปไว้หลังศีรษะ อาสนะ Dwi Pada Sirsasana แรกๆ ก็ยังไม่สบายตัว เธอถามว่า "สักวันมันจะดีขึ้นไหม?" เราตอบว่า "ดีขึ้นสิ... ด้วยความอดทน"

การเอาขาไว้หลังศีรษะต้องใช้เวลา บางคนต้องใช้ 2 หรือ 3 ปี กว่าจะเริ่มรู้สึกสบาย บางคนใช้นานกว่านั้นมาก จนเริ่มรู้สึกว่าไม่มีทาง คุณอาจรู้จักความรู้สึกนี้ดี จากอาสนะที่คุณกำลังติดอยู่ตอนนี้

เธอยังฝึกตามปกติ ไม่หยุด และช่วงหลังมานี้ เรารู้สึกได้ว่าเธอทำอาสนะนี้ได้ง่ายขึ้นมาก

แล้วจะไปต่อยังไง ในวันที่รู้สึกว่าไม่มีทาง?

เรื่องนี้พามาถึงนิสัยหนึ่งข้อ ที่ถ้าให้คุณได้แค่ข้อเดียว ก็จะให้ข้อนี้:

**ฝึกในเวลาเดิมทุกเช้า**

นาฬิกาปลุก ชาหรือกาแฟ เนาลิ (หมุนกล้ามเนื้อหน้าท้อง) ขึ้นเสื่อ แค่นั้นเอง

คุณไม่ได้ต้องการแรงจูงใจเพิ่ม แต่ต้องการเรื่องให้ตัดสินใจน้อยลง และครูที่สังเกตเห็นและทักถาม เวลาคุณไม่ได้มาชาลา พอเวลาฝึกตายตัวแล้ว ก็ไม่เหลืออะไรให้ต้องตัดสินใจ

ถ้าฟังดูเหมือนเรากำลังทำเรื่องเล็กให้เป็นเรื่องใหญ่ ก็ใช่เลย ยังไม่เคยเจออะไรที่สำคัญกว่านี้

อีกอย่างนะ "ทุกเช้า" หมายถึงทุกวันที่ฝึก ไม่นับวันเสาร์หรือวันมูนเดย์ และไม่ได้หมายถึงต้องฝึกครบทั้งซีรีส์ วันไหนที่แย่ๆ แค่สุริยนมัสการไม่กี่รอบก็นับแล้ว สิ่งที่เป็นนิสัยคือเวลา ส่วนจะฝึกยาวแค่ไหน เปลี่ยนได้

**สัปดาห์นี้ ลองทำแบบนี้ดู:** เลือกมาหนึ่งเวลา แล้วอยู่บนเสื่อให้ตรงเวลานั้น ใน 3 วันฝึกถัดไป แค่นั้นพอ

แล้วเจอกันบนเสื่อ
บุญชู

ป.ล. อย่าลืมนะ เราทุกคนกำลัง "แก่" ขึ้นทุกวัน และเราอยากรักษาพลังกับความแข็งแรงไว้ไปอีกนานๆ (อยากพูดได้จังว่าเรากำลังเด็กลง)

ป.ป.ล. วันจันทร์หน้า: "ตัวอ่อนพอไหม?" คำตอบตรงๆ จากเรา
```

## Chinese · WeChat article (with the Foolish Old Man paragraph)
```
Subject: 为什么我要每周给你写一封信

[星期几]，[几点]。[你在哪里写这封信。]

亲爱的朋友：

这是第一封信，后面还会有很多封。每周，我会给你写一件事，一件20年的阿斯汤加教会我的事。其中18年，我都是一周练六天。

练习。呼吸。休息。早起。卡住的时候。每周一封。三分钟读完。

这些信不会写得很精致。我宁愿每周给你写一封，也不想一年才写一封完美的信。

那就先从一位学生说起吧。她跟我练习，快十年了。

几个月前，她开始把两条腿都放到头后面。Dwi Pada Sirsasana，双腿绕头式。一开始并不舒服。她问我：“这个到底会不会好起来？”我说：“会的……只要你有耐心。”

腿绕头，需要时间。有的人要两三年，才刚开始觉得舒服一点。有的人要久得多，久到开始觉得：根本不可能。也许你现在正在练的某个体式，也让你有过这种感觉。

她每天的练习一直没停。最近，我能感觉到，她做起来轻松多了。

当你觉得根本不可能的时候，要怎么坚持下去？

中国有个老故事，叫愚公移山。愚公快九十岁了，想把家门前的两座大山搬走。他一筐一筐地把土石运走。有个叫智叟的老人笑他。愚公回答：“就算我死了，还有儿子，还有孙子，子子孙孙会一直挖下去。山却不会再长高。”最后，天帝派夸娥氏的两个儿子，把山背走了。

这就说到了我想送你的那个习惯。如果只能送你一个，我会送这个：

**每天早上，在同一个时间练习。**

闹钟。茶或咖啡。Nauli（瑙力），滚动腹部肌肉。上垫。就这些。

你需要的不是更多动力。你需要的是少做一些决定，还有一位老师。你没来道场，他会发现，也会问你。时间定下来了，就没什么要再决定的了。

如果你觉得我在小题大做，没错，我就是。我还没找到比这更重要的事。

顺便说一下，“每天早上”是指每个练习日，不包括周六和月亮日。也不是说一定要练完整个序列。状态不好的日子，做几个拜日式也算。时间，才是习惯。长短可以变。

**这周，试试这个：**选一个时间。接下来的三个练习日，都在这个时间站上垫子。就这些。

垫子上见，
Boonchu

P.S. 记住，我们都在一天天变“老”。我们都想让自己的精力和力量，保持得久一点，再久一点。（真希望我们能说，自己在一天天变年轻。）

P.P.S. 下周一聊：“我的柔韧性够吗？”我会老实回答。
```
Type under the photo:

```
想请你帮个小忙。给这个公众号发条消息，写两件事：你在哪个城市，你练了几年。一句话就够了。
```

## Russian · Telegram groups
```
Subject: Почему я буду писать вам каждую неделю

[День / Day], [время / time]. [Где я пишу / Where you are writing.]

Дорогой друг,

Это первое из многих писем. Каждую неделю я буду писать вам про одну вещь, которой меня научили 20 лет аштанги. Из них 18 лет я практикую шесть дней в неделю.

Практика. Дыхание. Отдых. Ранние подъёмы. Застой. Одно письмо в неделю. Три минуты на чтение.

Эти письма я не буду шлифовать. Лучше я буду писать вам каждую неделю, чем одно идеальное письмо в год.

Итак, начну с ученицы, которая практикует у меня почти 10 лет.

Несколько месяцев назад она начала закидывать обе ноги за голову. Дви Пада Ширшасана. Сначала было неудобно. Она спросила меня: «Когда-нибудь станет лучше?» Мой ответ: «Станет... с вашим терпением».

Ноги за голову требуют времени. Некоторым нужно 2 или 3 года, чтобы хотя бы начать чувствовать себя в этой асане комфортно. Другим гораздо больше, и им начинает казаться, что ничего не получится. Возможно, вам знакомо это чувство по асане, с которой вы сейчас боретесь.

Она не бросала свой режим. И в последнее время я чувствую, что ей становится гораздо легче.

Как продолжать, когда кажется, что ничего не получится?

И тут я подхожу к одной привычке. Если бы можно было дать вам только одну, то вот она:

**Практикуйте каждое утро в одно и то же время.**

Будильник. Чай или кофе. Наули, вращение мышц живота. Коврик. Вот и всё.

Вам не нужно больше мотивации. Вам нужно меньше решений. И учитель, который замечает, когда вас нет в шале, и спрашивает, где вы. Когда время постоянное, решать уже нечего.

Если вам кажется, что я придаю мелочи большое значение, то да, придаю. Ничего важнее мне найти не удалось.

Кстати, «каждое утро» значит каждый день практики, кроме суббот и лунных дней. И это не значит, что нужна вся серия. В плохой день засчитываются и несколько кругов Сурья Намаскар. Время и есть привычка. Длительность может меняться.

**На этой неделе попробуйте вот что:** выберите одно время. В ближайшие 3 дня практики будьте на коврике именно в это время. И всё.

До встречи на коврике,
Бунчу

P.S. Помните: мы все «стареем», и нам хочется надолго сохранить энергию и силу. (Жаль, что нельзя сказать, что мы молодеем.)

P.P.S. В следующий понедельник: «Хватает ли мне гибкости?» Мой честный ответ.
```


# Reel: Flexible or Stiff? (Lane B, fear buster "I'm not flexible")

Lane B · last posts: Small Steps (B), Ep. 4 bird (A), Ep. 5 Foolish Old Man coming (A) · Lane B is due.
Toolkit pick: Triple Hook (Contradiction) + 3-second blocks (Missy Elliott method) + Gervais close on Boonchu himself.
Why: everybody thinks flexible is the gift. You flip it: stiff people get the real benefit. Then the last joke is on you.

## OPENING HOOK (0-3 s), top 3 from 10 tested
1. PICK (Triple Hook): "Everybody want naturally flexible body. But the real benefit? Stiff people." | On screen: THE REAL BENEFIT? STIFF PEOPLE. (gold: STIFF) | Two mats side by side in Paschimottanasana: one folds flat, one (in front) hands at the shins. Hard cut to the stiff one on "Stiff people".
2. Pattern interrupt: (no music, only ujjayi breath) "'I'm not flexible.' Okay. You learn every part of your body." | On screen: "I'M NOT FLEXIBLE." OKAY. | Close-up of fingertips stopping a hand short of the toes, then cut to your face on "Okay".
3. Contrarian: "Everybody want flexible body. But stiff people get the real benefit." | On screen: STIFF BODY, REAL BENEFIT | You to camera, a flexible fold behind you, whip-pan to a stiff fold.

## TELEPROMPTER (read this, one take, about 38 s) · Hook 1 chosen by Boonchu, 4 Oct
Everybody want naturally flexible body.
But the real benefit?
Stiff people.

Natural flexible... it come with a problem.
When they need strength...
they might not control the flexibility.

And stiff people?
You think: I'm not flexible.
You must learn to bend. Every part of your body.
You go the hard way...
But you absorb every bit.
Every move. Every improvement.
Then you know... and you understand every stiff people.

That's the real benefit.
(1 s silence)

So which one you want?
Natural flexible... or tight, then learn to bend?
Let me know.

For me... I also wanna be naturally flexible.
(small beat)
I will deal with the strength later.
(hold 1 s)

## SHOT LIST (film these; every student says OK first, no names)
A. Two mats side by side, Paschimottanasana, side view, 10 s: flexible student folds flat, stiff student in front, hands at the shins. Both calm. (Used at 0-4 s and in the split screen at 28-32 s.)
B. You, talking head, the whole teleprompter in one take, shala background.
C. Students working at their edge, 2 s each: hips, hamstrings, shoulders, back.
D. You kneel and give the stiff student a gentle adjustment, both smiling, 5 s.
E. Close-up: the stiff student's hands slide a little closer to the feet (real, same session).
F. The same stiff student, smiling, a bit deeper, 3 s.
Made in the edit: plum card "FLEXIBLE + STRENGTH = ?".

## EDIT CUE SHEET (house style)
- Zoom punch about 1 s + SFX at 40%, 0.4 s after the word: Stiff people, problem, control, I'm not flexible, every part, hard way, absorb, understand, real benefit, naturally flexible (you), later.
- Captions: Weight Shift with emoji. On-screen text per block as in the table above.
- Colors: plum base, ONE gold word per frame (STIFF, REAL BENEFIT), teal for small labels.
- Cover: your face + "FLEXIBLE OR STIFF?" (gold: STIFF). Check in black and white.
- Speed: if your take runs over 40 s, speed 1.2x and cut pauses. Keep the 1 s silence after "real benefit" and the 1 s hold at the end.

| Time | You say | On screen | Picture |
|---|---|---|---|
